<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a pointwise 2-converse for elliptic curves over $`\mathbf Q`$ with nonzero rational two-torsion: if the $`2^\infty`$-Selmer corank is zero or one, then the analytic rank and Mordell–Weil rank equal that corank, and the Shafarevich–Tate group is finite. The result allows arbitrary reduction at 2.

- **claim-two-converse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $E/\mathbb Q$ be an elliptic curve with $E(\mathbb Q)[2]\ne0$ and $s_2(E)\in\{0,1\}$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(E,s)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}E(\mathbb Q)=s_2(E), \qquad \operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)\text{ is finite}.$$
  - Explanation: For a rational elliptic curve with nonzero rational two-torsion and Selmer corank zero or one, analytic rank, Mordell-Weil rank and Selmer corank coincide and the Shafarevich-Tate group is finite.
- **claim-three-ranks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pr-ranks)): There is an exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow A(\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb Q_2/\mathbb Z_2 \longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(A/\mathbb Q) \longrightarrow \operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty]\longrightarrow0.$$ Consequently $$s_2(A)=r(A)+\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty].$$ If $a(A)\in\{0,1\}$, then $r(A)=a(A)$ and the whole group $\operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)$ is finite. In that case $s_2(A)=a(A)$.
  - Explanation: The Kummer sequence relates Mordell-Weil rank, Selmer corank and the corank of the Shafarevich-Tate two-primary part; low analytic rank forces finiteness.
- **claim-parity-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pr-invariance)): For every elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$, $$(-1)^{s_2(A)}=\varepsilon(A).$$ A rational isogeny preserves $a$, $r$, and $s_2$. If $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$ is quadratic, then $$s_2(A/K)=s_2(A/\mathbb Q)+s_2(A^{(k)}/\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: Selmer parity equals the root number, rational isogenies preserve the three ranks, and Selmer coranks add over a quadratic field.
- **claim-cyclotomic-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cy-lower-bound)): There is a constant $C_E$ such that, for every allowed $h$ with $L(E^{(h)},1)\ne0$, $$v_2(\mathcal L(h))\ \geq\ 2w(h)+s(h)-C_E.$$ The constant is independent of the number and sizes of the primes dividing $h$.
  - Explanation: A uniform constant bounds from below the two-adic valuation of the normalized central value in terms of the weight and Shafarevich-Tate length, independent of the primes in the twist.
- **claim-cyclotomic-missing-vertex** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cy-missing-vertex)): Let $h_0$ be an allowed discriminant with $s_2(E^{(h_0)})=0$. Suppose that for arbitrarily large $b$ there are a finite set $Q$ of distinct primes $q\nmid 2Nh_0$ and linear forms $\lambda_q:\mathbb F _2^b\longrightarrow\mathbb F _2$ giving $$h_x=h_0\prod_{q\in Q}(q^*)^{\lambda_q(x)},\qquad x\in\mathbb F _2^b,$$ all in the fixed local and sign classes. Suppose that a number $B$, independent of the family, satisfies $$L(E^{(h_x)},1)\ne0,\qquad D(h_x)\leq B\qquad(x\ne0).$$ Then $L(E^{(h_0)},1)\ne0$ and $D(h_0)\leq B+C_E$. Here $C_E$ is independent of $h_0$, $Q$, and $b$; the dimension needed in the proof is allowed to depend on the fixed precision being tested.
  - Explanation: Bounds at every nonzero address of a binary family of twists transfer to the base vertex, giving nonvanishing and the same bound up to a fixed constant.
- **claim-integral-fourier** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cy-fourier)): Suppose $u\in\mathcal O[1/2][G]$, all $u_x$ belong to $\Lambda[1/2]$, and $2^C u\in\mathcal O[G]$. Then $2^C u\in\Lambda[G]$.
  - Explanation: Integrality over the completion together with membership of all character coordinates in the localized power series ring gives integrality over the power series group ring.
- **claim-binary-congruences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cy-binary)): Let $z_1,\ldots,z_r\in\mathbb Z _2[G]$. Given $M\geq1$, if $b>r(2^M-1)$, there is an $x\ne0$ such that $$(z_j)_x\equiv(z_j)_0\pmod {2^M}\qquad(1\leq j\leq r).$$ The bound depends on the number of tests and their precision, and not on the group-ring coefficients.
  - Explanation: On a sufficiently large Boolean cube a nonzero address agrees with the origin modulo a prescribed power of two for finitely many group ring elements, by a parity count of common zeros.
- **claim-perfect-real-class** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cy-perfect)): The complex <a href="#cy:positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cy:positive">[cy:positive]</a> is perfect over $R$ and computes derived coefficient specializations. There is a class $a\in H^1(C_S^+(\mathbb T))$ whose rational span is the real-place summand. Its integral index in that summand depends only on $E$ and the fixed real sign.
  - Explanation: The positive global complex is perfect, computes derived specializations and carries a real-place class of bounded index.
- **claim-residual-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cy-residual)): There is an isomorphism in the derived category $$C_S^+(\mathbb T)\otimes_R\mathcal O[G] \simeq \mathcal O[G]^2[-1].$$
  - Explanation: After tensoring to the local group ring the positive complex is concentrated in one degree with a free module of rank two.
- **claim-central-determinant** (bound, [source](#stmt-cy-central)): There is a constant $C_{\mathrm{loc}}$, depending only on the fixed curve and local classes, with the following property. If $s_2(E^{(h_x)})=0$, then $u_x(0)=0$ when $L(E^{(h_x)},1)=0$. Otherwise $$\label{cy:central-formula} \bigl\|v_2(u_x(0))-D(h_x)\bigr\|\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}.$$ The rational positive complex at $t=0$ has cohomology only in degree one, of dimension two, so these are specializations of the same determinant.
  - Explanation: At corank zero the central valuation of the determinant coordinate matches the arithmetic defect up to a fixed local constant, and vanishes with the central value.
- **claim-ring-class-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-rd-ring-lower)): Every simple-zero ring instance in the stated data satisfies $$2j_h\ge 4w(h)+s(h)+s(hk)-C(E,A), \qquad \omega(k)\le A. \label{rd:ring-lower-equation}$$
  - Explanation: Every simple-zero ring instance has Heegner index bounded below by weight and Shafarevich-Tate lengths, with a constant depending on the curve and the prime count of the companion.
- **claim-ring-missing-vertex** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rd-missing-vertex)): Fix the ring data $E,K$, with $$\operatorname{corank}_{\mathbb Z_2} \operatorname{Sel}_{2^\infty}(E/K)=1. \label{rd:base-corank}$$ Suppose binary families <a href="#rd:binary-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rd:binary-data">[rd:binary-data]</a> exist for arbitrarily large $b$, and for every nonzero $x$ the product $L(E^{(h_x)},s)L(E^{(h_xk)},s)$ has a simple zero and $$2j_{h_x}-4w(h_x)-s(h_x)-s(h_xk)\le B \label{rd:nonzero-bound}$$ with $B$ independent of the family. Then $$\operatorname{ord}_{s=1} \bigl(L(E,s)L(E^{(k)},s)\bigr)=1. \label{rd:missing-conclusion}$$ This implication does not assume Mordell–Weil rank one or finite Shafarevich–Tate group at the base.
  - Explanation: With a fixed imaginary quadratic field of Selmer corank one, bounded Heegner indices at nonzero addresses force a simple zero of the product of L-functions at the base.
- **claim-finite-artin-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-rl-finite-model)): Let $(A,\mathfrak m,k)$ be an Artin local ring, and let $D$ be a complex of flat $A$-modules. Suppose $H^*(D\otimes_A k)$ is finite dimensional and supported in $[a,b]$. There is a bounded finite free complex $C$, with $$\operatorname{rank}_A C^j=\dim_k H^j(D\otimes_A k),$$ and a strong deformation retraction $$i:C\longrightarrow D,\qquad p:D\longrightarrow C,\qquad pi=1,\qquad 1-ip=dh+hd.$$ Thus maps to the original complex, as well as maps between finite models, are available. Any fixed finite collection of chain maps, homotopies, triangles, and perfect pairings can be transported to these models.
  - Explanation: A complex of flat modules over an Artin local ring has a bounded finite free model with a strong deformation retraction onto the original complex.
- **claim-limits-of-diagrams** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-finite-limits)): Suppose diagrams over $A_i$, or over $A_i[G]$, have uniformly bounded cohomological amplitudes and graded free ranks, and include the chain homotopies and inverses for every asserted identity or equivalence. After taking a cofinal subsequence, or a nonprincipal ultrafilter limit, their matrices define the same diagram of perfect complexes over $\Lambda$, or $\Lambda[G]$.* *Moreover, the contractions into the original cochains may be retained for the evaluation construction in Lemma <a href="#rl:evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:evaluation">19</a>. Adding finitely many new objects or maps does not require changing already chosen models for the old objects.
  - Explanation: Uniformly bounded diagrams over the finite quotients have an ultrafilter limit giving the same diagram of perfect complexes over the power series ring, retaining contractions.
- **claim-integral-kummer-condition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-kummer)): Let $F$ be a finite extension of a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, and let $B/F$ be any elliptic curve. Put $M_F=B(F)^\wedge_2=\varprojlim_n B(F)/2^nB(F)$. There is a perfect local condition $$U_F=M_F[-1]\longrightarrow R\Gamma(F,T_2B).$$ For every $n\geq1$, derived reduction has $$H^0(U_F\otimes^{\mathbf L}\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)[2^n],\qquad H^1(U_F\otimes^{\mathbf L}\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)/2^nB(F).$$ Its map in degree zero is the isomorphism onto $H^0(F,B[2^n])$, and its map in degree one is the finite Kummer injection. The condition is its own exact orthogonal complement under local Tate duality. These assertions hold at $F\mid2$, for all reduction types, and without a condition on $B[2]$.
  - Explanation: The completed local point group gives a perfect local Kummer condition, self-orthogonal under local duality, for every reduction type including at the prime two.
- **claim-ring-class-characters** (construction, [source](#stmt-rl-characters)): Fix finitely many split primes of $K$ above odd rational primes, outside $2N$, and orient one prime in each conjugate pair. There are distinct split rational primes $r_i$ avoiding all the fixed data and characters $$\xi_i:\operatorname{Gal}(H_{r_i}/K)\longrightarrow \mathbf Z/2^{n_i}\mathbf Z, \qquad n_i\longrightarrow\infty,$$ such that $\xi_i$ is surjective on the inertia quotient at $r_i$, is zero at the primes above $2N$, and has odd value at each prescribed oriented prime. We can require $r_i\to1$ in $\mathbf Z_2$ and $$\label{rl:trace-bound} v_2(2+a_{r_i}(E)),\quad v_2(2-a_{r_i}(E)),\quad v_2\det(1\pm\operatorname{Fr}_{r_i}\mid T)\ \leq c_E.$$ The constant $c_E$ is independent of the list and of $K$.
  - Explanation: Chebotarev realizes moving ring-class characters with prescribed odd values at oriented primes and uniformly bounded Frobenius determinant valuations.
- **claim-local-height-one-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-local-acyclic)): For the individual coefficient system $T\chi_h\Psi$, use full local conditions at the moving prime $r$ and the active primes dividing $h$, without adding primes from a larger binary family. The limiting full local complexes are acyclic over every height-one residue field of $\Lambda$ of characteristic zero. At $(2)$, with residue field $\Omega=\mathbf F_2((t))$, the $r$-complex is acyclic, and so are the complexes at active split primes whose prescribed exponent is odd. These full conditions therefore agree with their orthogonal conditions on the stated tests.
  - Explanation: Full local complexes at the moving and active primes are acyclic at characteristic zero height-one residue fields and at the prime two, so full and orthogonal conditions agree.
- **claim-uniform-residual-sizes** (bound, [source](#stmt-rl-residual-bound)): Suppose $\omega(\operatorname{disc}K)\leq A$, the local twisting classes at $2N$ belong to a fixed finite list, and all active conductor primes are split with odd exponent. After localization and completion at $(2)$, the minimal free ranks of the Selmer complexes are bounded in terms of $E,A$, and the number of retained derivative primes. They do not depend on the number of active conductor primes.
  - Explanation: Minimal free ranks of the completed Selmer complexes are bounded independently of the number of active conductor primes.
- **claim-all-sequence-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-evaluation)): Fix $K$ and a sequence of the finite cochain constructions above. Keep the contraction maps into the original global cochains. Let $\Gamma_K=\prod_i G_K$ and $\Gamma=\Gamma_K\rtimes\langle\tau\rangle\subset\prod_iG_{\mathbf Q}$, with simultaneous complex conjugation $\tau$. Thus $\Gamma_K$ has index two in $\Gamma$; the larger unrestricted product of rational Galois groups is not used for induction. The matrix limit defines a coefficient action of $\Gamma_K$ on $\Lambda^2$. For every field $L$ receiving a map from $\Lambda$, there is a natural injection $$H^1(C\otimes_\Lambda L)\longrightarrow H^1_{\rm abs}(\Gamma_K,L^2),$$ where the right side denotes abstract crossed-homomorphism classes. Degree-zero evaluation likewise injects $H^0(C\otimes_\Lambda L)$ into $(L^2)^{\Gamma_K}$. Here $C$ may be the global complex or a Selmer complex whose local condition maps induce isomorphisms on local $H^0$ and injections on local $H^1$. The same statement holds for the induced representation of $\Gamma$.* *There are evaluation maps on arbitrary sequences, not just on the finite list used to detect cohomology. Their degree-two identities remain valid after arbitrary coefficient extension.
  - Explanation: Cohomology injects into abstract crossed-homomorphism classes and degree-zero invariants, with evaluations defined on arbitrary sequences of group elements.
- **claim-exact-common-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-kernel)): In the notation of Lemma <a href="#rl:evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:evaluation">19</a>, let $H$ be the group of sequences each of whose entries acts trivially on the full Tate module, on all ring-class fields of $K$, and on the quadratic characters in use. Restriction from the crossed classes in that Lemma to $H$ is injective at every characteristic-zero test. It is also injective at the residual $(2)$ test. The characteristic-zero annihilator needed for this assertion can be chosen with a fixed finite $2$-valuation depending only on $E$, also as $K$ varies.
  - Explanation: Restriction of crossed classes to the exact common kernel is injective at characteristic zero tests and at the residual test, using a central scalar.
- **claim-outer-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-outer-evaluation)): Let $C_j$ be any family of the preceding limiting complexes, completed over $\mathcal O_j=\widehat{\Lambda_{(2)}}$, possibly for different quadratic fields $K_j$. Suppose their minimal ranks are bounded. Keep their actual evaluation matrices, and transport them through the integral contractions to these minimal models. For a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\mathcal U$, put $$\mathcal O_\infty= \left(\prod_{\mathcal U}\mathcal O_j\right)/\mathfrak a, \qquad \mathfrak a=\{(x_j): \{j:v_2(x_j)\geq n\}\in\mathcal U\text{ for every }n\}.$$ Then $\mathcal O_\infty$ is a DVR with uniformizer $2$ and a characteristic-zero fraction field $L_\infty$. The outer complex has an injection of its $H^1$ over $L_\infty$ into crossed classes on the product of the inner sequence groups. Its restriction to the product of their exact common kernels is injective. All arbitrary-sequence degree-two evaluation identities survive. These conclusions also hold after retaining any fixed finite collection of additional integral diagrams.
  - Explanation: The ultraproduct quotient is a discrete valuation ring with uniformizer two and the outer cohomology injects into crossed classes, including on the exact kernels.
- **claim-irreducibility-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-irreducibility)): On an inner or outer characteristic-zero test where $\Psi^2\ne1$, the four-dimensional representation induced from $T\chi\Psi$ is absolutely irreducible. If $\Psi^2=1$ on an inner test, it splits as two inequivalent absolutely irreducible quadratic twists. In the outer test $\Psi$ has infinite order.
  - Explanation: The induced representation is absolutely irreducible when the moving character is not quadratic, and splits into two inequivalent quadratic twists otherwise.
- **claim-chebotarev-evaluation** (construction, [source](#stmt-rl-chebotarev-evaluation)): For any element $u$ of an exact kernel in Lemma <a href="#rl:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:kernel">20</a>, derivative primes can be chosen, at increasing finite precision, whose rational Frobenius is $\tau u$ on all retained data. Their finite localization is evaluation on $(\tau u)^2$. The assertion is compatible with the outer construction of Proposition <a href="#rl:outer-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:outer-evaluation">21</a> and with any fixed finite list of previous derivative operations.
  - Explanation: Derivative primes can be chosen at increasing precision with prescribed Frobenius so that finite localization is evaluation on the square of the chosen element.
- **claim-limit-evaluation-package** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rl-limit-evaluation-package)): For the ring data fixed at the start of this section, retain the finite target-side arithmetic diagrams with their maps to original cochains, homotopies, and dualities as in Lemma <a href="#rl:finite-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:finite-limits">14</a>. The moving characters, local conditions, and evaluation constructions then have the following simultaneous properties:* 1. *Every specified finite target-side arithmetic diagram admits a bounded free model together with maps to its original cochains; the specified maps, homotopies and dualities survive the inner limit.* 2. *At the stated height-one tests the relaxed active local complexes are acyclic, and the Selmer duality pairs degrees one and two. Integral Kummer conditions at $2N$ include their full derived degree-zero invariants.* 3. *The residual sizes needed for the outer construction are bounded independently of the active conductor support, with dependence only on $E$, the bound on $\omega(\operatorname{disc}K)$, and the number of retained derivative primes.* 4. *Inner and outer $H^1$ are detected by actual crossed cocycles, including on the exact common kernel. The evaluations and their degree-two identities can be realized by finite Chebotarev conditions without disturbing earlier finite data.* *These assertions do not assert an Euler-system reciprocity law or an integral derivative class that has not first been constructed at finite level. Once those finite arithmetic diagrams are constructed, this Theorem supplies their limit compatibility.
  - Explanation: The finite arithmetic diagrams, acyclicity, residual bounds and evaluations hold simultaneously and survive the limits.
- **claim-split-local-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-local-model)): Let a sequence of inert good primes $\ell$ have rational Frobenius lifts tending on $T$ to a fixed complex conjugation $J$. At the unique prime of $K$ above $\ell$, the limiting local complex has $$H^0=M,\qquad H^1=F\oplus S=M\oplus M,\qquad H^2=M, \label{rd:local-cohomology}$$ with zero differentials in a free split model. Here $M=T\otimes\Lambda$ with the relevant scalar coefficient structure. The two degree-one coordinates of a cocycle are $$f(c)=c(\gamma^2),\qquad s(c)=c(\sigma), \label{rd:coordinates}$$ where $\gamma$ is the chosen rational Frobenius lift and $\sigma$ is an oriented pro-$2$ tame generator. The plane conditions $F=\{s=0\}$ and $S=\{f=0\}$ include $H^0$ and exclude $H^2$. Each is its own orthogonal complement for conjugate-Weil duality. The strict condition $H^0$ and the relaxed condition $H^0\oplus H^1[-1]$ are mutually dual. All four inclusions can be equipped with compatible integral duality triangles.
  - Explanation: At inert good primes the limiting local complex splits with isotropic finite and singular planes that are their own orthogonal complements.
- **claim-derivative-switch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-derivative)): Perform each finite ring-class construction and its weighted transfer before retaining its target-side data by the construction following Lemma <a href="#rl:finite-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:finite-limits">14</a>. There is one integer $L\ne0$, depending on the fixed curve, parametrization and fixed local types, such that the classes obtained from the Heegner derivatives of $L$ times the point satisfy, for every finite switched set $I$, $$P_I\in H^1(C_I),\qquad f(P_{I\cup\{\ell\}})=0,\qquad s(P_{I\cup\{\ell\}})=J f(P_I). \label{rd:switch}$$ The same $L$ works for every number of active and derivative primes. The previously imposed singular conditions are retained.
  - Explanation: A single integer multiplier makes the descended Heegner derivative classes satisfy the switch relation between finite and singular coordinates for every switched set.
- **claim-determinant-switch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-det-switch)): Let $O$ be a DVR with fraction field $F_O$. Suppose that integral perfect complexes $C_{\rm str},C_F,C_S,C_{\rm rel}$, their localization triangles, and their dualities are the base changes of the diagram in Lemma <a href="#rd:local-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:local-model">25</a>. Suppose the generic cohomology of these four global complexes is concentrated in degrees one and two, that $H^1(C_F\otimes F_O)=F_OA$, and $$f(A)=v_0\ne0,\qquad s(B)=Jv_0,\qquad f(B)=0$$ for a class $B$ in the switched complex. The determinant tensors are formed from each class and its functional under these same dualities. Then the switched generic cohomology has rank one, is spanned by $B$, and $$v_O(u_F)=v_O(u_S). \label{rd:valuation-switch}$$ Neither localization is required to be primitive.
  - Explanation: Switching the local condition preserves the valuation of the determinant coordinate over a discrete valuation ring without primitivity of localizations.
- **claim-rank-two-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-rank-two)): Let $\mathcal G$ be a group, $H$ a normal subgroup acting trivially on an absolutely irreducible representation $W$ over a field $F_O$, and suppose that restriction of the relevant cohomology classes to $H$ is injective. For two independent classes, their joint evaluations on $H$ span $W\oplus W$. If the finite localization is the rank-two projection supplied by $1+\tau$ on the induced representation, some actual evaluation has rank two. One of the two classes may be any prescribed nonzero class.
  - Explanation: Independent classes have joint evaluation spanning two copies of the irreducible representation, so some actual evaluation has full rank.
- **claim-valuation-away-from-two** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-away-two)): Assume the simultaneous diagrams, derivative classes, and exact-kernel evaluation maps of Lemmas <a href="#rl:evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:evaluation">19</a>, <a href="#rl:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:kernel">20</a>, <a href="#rd:local-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:local-model">25</a>, and <a href="#rd:derivative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:derivative">26</a>. For a primitive constituent whose generic cohomology has rank at most one in degrees one and two and zero elsewhere, its class-dual determinant coordinate belongs to $\Lambda[1/2]$. This includes a simple-zero constituent and a base with $2^\infty$-Selmer corank one.
  - Explanation: The class-dual determinant coordinate lies in the power series ring with two inverted, by rank reduction at each height-one prime other than two.
- **claim-terminal-minor** (bound, [source](#stmt-rd-minor)): Let $$C_i=[\,O_i^n\xrightarrow{D_i}O_i^n\,]$$ be based complexes over DVRs with uniformizer $2$, with generic rank-one kernel. Let $z_i\in\ker D_i$ be integral, and let $\lambda_i\in 2^{-a}(O_i^n)^*$ annihilate $\operatorname{im}D_i$, where $a$ is fixed. If an $(n-1)$-minor $\Delta_i$ has $v(\Delta_i)\le b$, the determinant coordinate $u_i$ of $z_i\otimes\lambda_i$ satisfies $$v(u_i)\ge-a-b. \label{rd:minor-bound}$$
  - Explanation: A bounded-valuation maximal minor of the differential bounds the determinant coordinate from below.
- **claim-outer-residual-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-rd-outer-bound)): Assume all of the following properties of a collection of primitive ring instances:* 1. *after any fixed number of switches, minimal $\mathcal O$-models have size bounded independently of $h$ and of $K$ with $\omega(k)\le A$;* 2. *the all-sequence evaluation and exact-kernel restriction of Proposition <a href="#rl:outer-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:outer-evaluation">21</a>, and the finite Chebotarev realization of Lemma <a href="#rl:chebotarev-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:chebotarev-evaluation">23</a>, hold for these models, retaining their degree-two cocycle identities;* 3. *the integral localization and duality diagrams and the derivative classes of Lemma <a href="#rd:derivative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:derivative">26</a> survive this same outer limit; the class and its closed dual functional have a common bounded clearing exponent.* *Then there is $C(E,A)$ such that $$v_{(2)}(u)\ge-C(E,A) \label{rd:residual-bound}$$ for every simple-zero ring instance. The hypotheses refer to a single simultaneous construction. In particular, separate abstract models realizing its individual cohomology groups do not establish this proposition’s arithmetic applicability.
  - Explanation: Under simultaneous diagram hypotheses, the residual valuation of the coordinate is bounded below uniformly over simple-zero ring instances.
- **claim-derived-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-derived-specialization)): Let $C$ be a perfect $\Lambda$-complex and put $C_0=C\otimes_\Lambda^{\mathbf L}\mathbb Z_2$, where $t=0$. Suppose its generic rational cohomology and its central rational cohomology both have dimension one in degrees one and two and are zero in other degrees. Put $A=\Lambda_{(t)}$, with residue field $\mathbb Q_2$. Let $z$ be a degree-one cycle and $\lambda$ a closed degree-two functional in a finite free model of $C\otimes_\Lambda A$, and let $z_0,\lambda_0$ be their images under $A\to\mathbb Q_2$. Then the generic determinant tensor $[z]\otimes[\lambda]$ is regular at $(t)$ and specializes, under the determinant base-change isomorphism, to $[z_0]\otimes[\lambda_0]$. Its central valuation is computed from the entire integral complex $C_0$, including its torsion cohomology. It cannot be computed by substituting the naive quotient of $H^1(C)$ by $t$.
  - Explanation: At constant rational rank the generic determinant tensor specializes to the central tensor, whose valuation uses the entire integral complex including torsion.
- **claim-active-local-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-active-local)): Let $F_v/\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified, $p\ne2$, and let $T'=T_2(E)\otimes\chi$, where $E$ has good reduction and $\chi$ is ramified quadratic. Then $$H^0(F_v,T')=0,\qquad H^1(F_v,T')=E^\chi(F_v)^{\wedge}_2,\qquad \operatorname{length}_{\mathbb Z_2}H^2(F_v,T') =\dim_{\mathbb F_2}E[2]^{\operatorname{Fr}_v}. \label{rd:local-length}$$ The quotient of the full local complex by the integral Kummer condition therefore contributes precisely this last length in degree two.
  - Explanation: At a ramified quadratic twist at a good odd place the local cohomology is computed and the degree-two length equals the dimension of Frobenius-fixed two-torsion.
- **claim-central-determinant-formula** (bound, [source](#stmt-rd-central-formula)): Suppose the product $$L(E^{(h)},s)L(E^{(hk)},s)$$ has a simple zero at $s=1$. Use the primitive Heegner sum $P_h$ and its free-line index $j_h$ defined at the start of Section <a href="#rl:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:section">4</a>, and put $$s(h)=\operatorname{length}_{\mathbb Z_2} \operatorname{Sha}(E^{(h)}/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty].$$ Use the primitive Selmer determinant coordinate $u_h$ with the fixed class multiplier of Lemma <a href="#rd:derivative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:derivative">26</a>. For the primitive Selmer diagrams constructed above, we have $$\begin{split} v(u_h(0)) &=2j_h- \operatorname{length}_{\mathbb Z_2} \operatorname{Sha}(E^{(h)}/K)[2^\infty] -2\sum_{p\mid h}t_p+O_E(1)\\ &=2j_h-s(h)-s(hk)-4w(h)+O_{E,A}(1), \qquad \omega(k)\le A . \end{split} \label{rd:central-value}$$ The first error includes the auxiliary prime and the fixed parametrization multiplier. The second has no dependence on the number of primes dividing $h$.
  - Explanation: The central valuation of the determinant equals twice the Heegner index minus Shafarevich-Tate lengths and weights, up to a bounded error.
- **claim-unused-primes** (construction, [source](#stmt-rd-unused)): Suppose the universal relaxed trace class and its duality diagram have been constructed over $R$. There is $\eta\in\mathcal O[G]$ such that $\eta u_{\rm rel}$ has each character coordinate equal to the primitive determinant coordinate, up to a $\Lambda$-unit. The correction has no denominator accumulating with $\#Q$.
  - Explanation: A correction in the group ring removes Euler factors at unused primes character by character without denominators growing with the prime count.
- **claim-laurent-elimination** (bound, [source](#stmt-rd-schur)): There is a natural identification $$\mathcal O/2^M\mathcal O =(\mathbb Z/2^M\mathbb Z)((t)). \label{rd:laurent}$$ Suppose $A=\operatorname{diag}(A_1,\ldots,A_m)$ has entries in $\Lambda$, each residual determinant is nonzero, and $\operatorname{ord}_t(\det\overline A_j)\le h$. If $B=A+2E$ with $E$ over $\Lambda$, then every entry of $B^{-1}\bmod2^M$ has no term below $t^{-Mh}$. The same bound holds for a Schur complement $P-QB^{-1}S$, where $P,Q,S$ have power-series entries. The bound is independent of $m$.
  - Explanation: Inverses and Schur complements modulo a power of two have negative Laurent support bounded independently of the number of blocks.
- **claim-clearing-bounded-complex** (construction, [source](#stmt-rd-clearing)): Let $D$ be a based complex of finite free $\mathcal O[G]$-modules of total graded rank at most $r_0$, with $D_0$ defined over $\Lambda$. Suppose $D_0\otimes_\Lambda^{\mathbf L}\mathbb Z_2$ has rational cohomology of dimension one in degrees one and two, zero elsewhere, and total integral torsion length at most $h_1$. Let $z$ be an integral cycle and let $\lambda\in2^{-a_0}(D^2)^*$ be a closed functional. Assume every character constituent is either generically acyclic or has the same generic rank-one pattern; in the generically acyclic case its determinant coordinate is defined to be zero. Multiplication of these coordinates by any $\eta\in\mathcal O[G]$ is permitted. Then there are $f,d\in\mathcal O[G]$ such that $$f_xu_x=d_x,\qquad f_0\in\Lambda,\qquad v(f_0(0))\le C(r_0,h_1,a_0)<\infty. \label{rd:clearing-equations}$$ If all entries of $D_x$ have bounded negative Laurent support at each precision, uniformly in $b$, the same is true of $f_x$. No such bound on $d_x,z,\lambda,\eta$ is required.
  - Explanation: A bounded complex admits clearing elements whose central valuation and pole order are bounded in terms of rank and torsion length.
- **claim-family-clearing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-family-clearing)): Assume the compatible universal Selmer and Heegner diagrams and their primitive specializations. Suppose every character constituent of the universal Selmer complex is generically acyclic or has generic cohomology of dimension one in degrees one and two and zero elsewhere. If the fixed base has $2^\infty$-Selmer corank one over $K$, the corrected coordinates of Lemma <a href="#rd:unused" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:unused">35</a> admit the factorization <a href="#rd:clearing-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rd:clearing-equations">[rd:clearing-equations]</a>, with central valuation and fixed-precision pole bounds independent of $b$ and $\#Q$.
  - Explanation: The universal Selmer coordinates of the binary family admit a clearing factor with bounds independent of cube dimension and prime support.
- **claim-diagram-assembly** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-assembly)): For the ring data stated at the start of this section, the actual finite Heegner and Selmer constructions satisfy the simultaneous diagram hypotheses of Propositions <a href="#rd:away-two" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:away-two">29</a>, <a href="#rd:outer-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:outer-bound">31</a>, and <a href="#rd:central-formula" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:central-formula">34</a>. The bounds needed for the lower-bound argument depend only on $E$, the fixed local data, and a bound $A$ on $\omega(k)$. For the binary data <a href="#rd:binary-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rd:binary-data">[rd:binary-data]</a> with fixed $E,K$ and base Selmer corank one, assume in addition that every character constituent of the universal Selmer complex is generically acyclic or has generic cohomology of dimension one in degrees one and two and zero elsewhere. Then the constructions satisfy all the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#rd:family-clearing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:family-clearing">38</a>, with bounds independent of the cube dimension and of its new-prime support.
  - Explanation: The actual Heegner and Selmer constructions satisfy the simultaneous diagram hypotheses needed by the propositions on valuation, residual bounds and central formula.
- **claim-prescribed-bits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-bits)): Let $A_1(x),\ldots,A_q(x)$ be specified Laurent coefficients of sign evaluations of elements of $\mathcal O[G]$. For $M\ge1$, if $$b>q(2^M-1), \label{rd:bit-threshold}$$ there is $x\ne0$ with $A_i(x)\equiv A_i(0)\pmod{2^M}$ for every $i$.
  - Explanation: On a large enough cube some nonzero address matches the origin in prescribed Laurent coefficients modulo a power of two.
- **claim-binary-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rd-binary-transfer)): Suppose $f,d\in\mathcal O[G]$, $u_x\in\Lambda[1/2]$, and $f_xu_x=d_x$ for every character. Suppose $f_0\in\Lambda$ and $c=v(f_0(0))<\infty$. If every $f_x\bmod2^M$ has no powers below $-L(M)$, then for $$M>c,\qquad b>(L(M)+2)(2^M-1)$$ there is $x\ne0$ satisfying $$v\bigl(u_x(0)-u_0(0)\bigr)\ge M-2c. \label{rd:binary-congruence}$$
  - Explanation: Through the bounded clearing factor, a nonzero vertex has central determinant coordinate congruent to the base one to high precision.
- **claim-local-witnesses** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nv-local-witness)): Fix an elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$ and a nonempty filter of fundamental discriminants prime to $2N_A$, with specified real sign and unit squareclasses at finitely many primes. If the root number in the filter is $+1$, it contains $d$ of arbitrarily large absolute value with $L(A^{(d)},1)\ne0$. If the root number is $-1$, it contains such $d$ with $L'(A^{(d)},1)\ne0$. Any additional finite set of primes may be excluded.
  - Explanation: Any nonempty filter of discriminants contains arbitrarily large twists with nonzero central value or nonzero derivative according to the root number.
- **claim-bounded-factor-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nv-rough)): There is an absolute integer $A_0$ with the following property. In the positive-root-number case of Proposition <a href="#nv:local-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:local-witness">43</a>, one can require $\omega(d)\le A_0$ and require every prime divisor of $d$ to exceed any fixed bound. The size threshold for the resulting $d$ may depend on the curve, the filter, and the excluded primes; $A_0$ does not.
  - Explanation: In the positive root number case the witness can be taken with an absolutely bounded number of prime factors, all larger than any fixed bound.
- **claim-fixed-progression-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-nv-moment)): There are absolute constants $\eta>0$ and $B>0$ such that the following assertions hold. For squarefree $b$ prime to $H$, put $$S_{j,b}(X)= \sum_{\substack{n>0\ {\rm squarefree}\\ n\equiv a\ (H)\\b\mid n}} L^{(j)}(1/2,f\otimes\chi_{\sigma n})\Phi(n/X).$$ If the common root number is $+1$, then $$S_{0,b}(X)=cX\prod_{p\mid b}\beta_p+ O_{f,H,\Phi}(b^B X^{1-\eta}), \qquad c>0,\quad 0<\beta_p<1,\quad \beta_p=\frac1p+O(p^{-2}).$$ The last implied constant is absolute. If the common root number is $-1$, then $$S_{1,1}(X)=cX\log X+c_1X+ O_{f,H,\Phi}(X^{1-\eta}),\qquad c>0.$$
  - Explanation: First moments of central values or derivatives over a fixed progression have a positive main term and a power-saving error polynomial in the imposed divisor.
- **claim-even-coefficient-system** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-even)): Fix an even-sign filter and a nonvanishing index $D_0$ in it. There is a holomorphic cuspidal form $g=\sum_{n\geq1}a(n)q^n$ of weight $3/2$, orthogonal to unary theta series, of level supported on a fixed enlargement of $S$, and characters $\eta,\psi$ of $2$-power order such that $\psi/\eta=\chi_{-\sigma}$ and $$\label{co:recurrence} a(p^2n)=\bigl(\eta(p)a_p(E)-\psi(p)(-n/p)\bigr)a(n) -p\eta(p)^2a(n/p^2)\qquad(p\notin S).$$ Its coefficients are zero off the unit-squareclass filter. After normalization at $D_0$, every squarefree coefficient is rational up to a $2$-power root of unity. For two nonzero squarefree coefficients, $$\label{co:exact-ratio} 2v\!\left(\frac{a(D)}{a(D_0)}\right) =v\!\left(\frac{L_*(\sigma D)}{L_*(\sigma D_0)}\right).$$ Moreover $a(D)\neq0$ exactly when $L(E^{(\sigma D)},1)\neq0$. Consequently any previously established bound $v(L_*(\sigma D))\geq2w(D)+s(\sigma D)-C$ gives $$\label{co:even-bound} v(a(D))\geq w(D)+\tfrac12s(\sigma D)-C_g$$ with $C_g$ fixed independently of $D$.
  - Explanation: A weight three-halves cusp form with Hecke recurrence has coefficients whose valuation ratios match those of normalized central values, giving a coefficient lower bound.
- **claim-kummer-detection** (construction, [source](#stmt-co-kummer)): Let $C$ act on $T=T_2E$ as complex conjugation, and choose a nonzero integral row $b:T\longrightarrow\mathbb Z_2$ with $bC=b$. For a fixed positive simple-zero witness $D_w$, there are arbitrarily large precisions $M$ and good primes $\ell$ such that $$\label{co:ell-conditions} \rho_T(\operatorname{Fr}_\ell)\equiv C\pmod {2^M},\qquad (h_*/\ell)=-1,\qquad(D_w/\ell)=1.$$ Evaluation at Frobenius squared defines an integral homomorphism $$\lambda_M:E(\mathbb F_{\ell^2})\longrightarrow\mathbb Z/2^M\mathbb Z, \qquad \lambda_M=b\kappa_M,$$ of Frobenius sign $+1$. The valuation of $\lambda_M(\operatorname{red}_\ell(LP_{D_w}))$ is bounded independently of $M$, for a fixed nonzero integer $L$ making the twist comparison integral. Extra fixed finite-field restrictions can be retained when they agree with the chosen complex-conjugation coset, or when a compatible lift has been specified.
  - Explanation: Chebotarev gives good primes with Frobenius congruent to complex conjugation and an integral evaluation functional detecting a nontorsion point with bounded loss.
- **claim-weighted-theta-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-theta-bridge)): For $\ell$ as in Lemma <a href="#co:kummer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:kummer">47</a>, the reduced modular parametrization followed by $\lambda_M$ admits an integral weighted ternary theta lift. After extraction of the coefficients at multiples of $\|h_*\|$ it gives a classical holomorphic weight-$3/2$ form $g_M=\sum a_M(n)q^n$, of level supported on $S\cup\{\ell\}$, with <a href="#co:recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="co:recurrence">[co:recurrence]</a> modulo $2^M$. The associated characters have $2$-power order and quadratic ratio. For squarefree admissible $D$, $$\label{co:weighted-comparison} a_M(D)=c_0\,2^{\omega(N)}\zeta_M(D) \lambda_M\bigl(\operatorname{red}_\ell(LP_D)\bigr) \pmod {2^M},$$ where $\zeta_M(D)$ is a $2$-power root of unity and $c_0,L$ are fixed integers independent of $D,M,\ell$. The extra index restriction at $\ell$ is $(D/\ell)=1$.
  - Explanation: A weighted ternary theta lift of the reduced parametrization yields a holomorphic form whose coefficients equal the Kummer functional on reduced Heegner points.
- **claim-odd-coefficient-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-odd)): The forms in Lemma <a href="#co:theta-bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:theta-bridge">48</a>, after a fixed adjustment of precision, satisfy $$v(a_M(D))\geq\min\{M,j(P_D)-C\},\qquad v(a_M(D))<M\ \Longrightarrow\ j(P_D)\leq v(a_M(D))+C.$$ A visible coefficient detects $a(E^{(D)})=1$, and a fixed positive witness has bounded coefficient valuation as $M\to\infty$. All constants are independent of $M,\ell,D$.
  - Explanation: Valuations of the odd coefficients are controlled by the Heegner index valuation, so a visible coefficient detects analytic order one.
- **claim-stabilized-minimum-depth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-minimum)): Assume the uniform arithmetic lower bounds just stated. Each coefficient source defines a binary symbol $$\label{co:symbol} A(D)=\begin{cases} 1,&v(a(D))=\delta+w(D),\\ 0,&v(a(D))>\delta+w(D)\text{ or }D\text{ is off the filter}, \end{cases}$$ with a fixed minimum normalized depth $\delta$ and at least one bounded-weight witness. For the odd source $a=a_M$ at an actual stage: the numerical minimum and its divisibility statements stabilize at every fixed finite weight bound. The finite symbol tables on arithmetic labels will be stabilized separately in Section <a href="#pw:odd-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-stages">9.5</a>. At a unit symbol, $$v(L_*(\sigma D))\leq2w(D)+O(1) \quad\text{or}\quad j(P_D)\leq w(D)+O(1),$$ respectively, and the analytic order is respectively zero or one.
  - Explanation: Under the arithmetic lower bounds each coefficient source gives a binary symbol with a fixed minimum normalized depth and a bounded-weight witness.
- **claim-coefficient-operators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-operators)): For the even source let $U$ be arbitrary. For the odd source, fix an integer $R\ge1$ and a weight bound $W$; all assertions below are made at a sufficiently advanced actual stage, simultaneously for every $U$ with $w(U)\le W$ and every Fourier index. In these respective settings the coefficients of $B_U$ are integral. Write uniquely $n=Jrm^2$, with $J\subseteq U$, $r$ squarefree, and $(r,U)=1$. The coefficient vanishes unless $D=(U/J)r$ belongs to the filter, and for the even source otherwise $$\label{co:BU-bound} v(B_U[n])\geq w(r)+\tfrac12\#\{p\in J:p\text{ simple}\}-e_U.$$ At that stage the odd source satisfies this inequality for every $U$ with $w(U)\le W$ and every $n$, with its right side replaced by its minimum with $R$. In particular the untruncated bound holds on any prescribed bounded range of squarefree weights, after choosing $R$ and the stage. For a split $p\in J$ the difference factor, after its unit phase and one factor of $2$ are removed, has residue $$\label{co:cp} c_p(D)=\frac{a_p(E)-(\psi/\eta)(p)(-D/p)-1}{2}\pmod2$$ at every square shell. For a simple $p$ this formula holds on the first shell $p\nmid m$.* *Choose $i\in\{0,1\}$ such that $\eta\chi_{-4}^i$ is odd, and set $$\Theta_J=\sum_{\substack{m>0\\(m,S)=1}} \eta(m)\chi_{-4}(m)^i m q^{Jm^2}.$$ For matching $U$ the modular form $$\label{co:HU} H_U=\frac12\left(B_U- \sum_{\substack{J\subseteq U\\J\text{ split only}}} B_U[J]\Theta_J\right)$$ has integral coefficients, with the same stage and weight restriction in the odd case. For opposite $U$ put $H_U=B_U$. The new primes in the level of $\Theta_J$ have exponent one, and its character is $\eta\chi_{-4}^i(-J/\cdot)$.
  - Explanation: Divided operators have integral coefficients with explicit valuation bounds and unary theta removal at the stated stage.
- **claim-integral-traces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-traces)): The module $\mathcal M$ is an integral Fourier lattice preserved by the trace and determinant operators. On the coefficientwise reduced trace orbit of the indicated vector, $$\label{co:trace-identities} d(u)t(v)=t(v),\qquad t(u)t(v)=t(uv)+t(uv^{-1}),\qquad t(1)=0.$$ Every coefficient test of a finite product of traces can be performed using distinct fresh primes avoiding the tested index. In particular testing $t(u_1)\cdots t(u_a)$ at $n$ becomes testing the original reduced coefficient at $nq_1\cdots q_a$.
  - Explanation: The lattice generated by the forms is preserved by trace and determinant operators, which satisfy the stated identities and reduce tests to fresh primes.
- **claim-level-prime-inertia** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-inertia)): For $p\in U$, choose inertia $\tau_p$ on which the new ramified quadratic character is $-1$, and an arithmetic Frobenius lift $\phi_p$ in the same decomposition group. In characteristic zero, $$\label{co:inertia-identity} U_p^{(2)}\bigl(t(g\tau_p\phi_p)-t(g\phi_p)\bigr) =d(\phi_p)\bigl(t(g\tau_p)-t(g)\bigr).$$ Here $U_p^{(2)}$ extracts $p$-multiples in weight two. The identity commutes with further good traces.
  - Explanation: An identity relates the level-prime operator to trace differences along ramified inertia and Frobenius lifts on all constituents.
- **claim-symbol-interface** (theorem, [source](#stmt-co-symbol-interface)): Under the arithmetic lower bounds in Lemma <a href="#co:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:minimum">50</a>, the symbols of either coefficient source have the following properties.* 1. *For matching $U$, the trace on split elements factors through the maximal elementary $2$-extension of $F$ unramified outside $S\cup U$. For opposite $U$, traces factor through the corresponding rational elementary extension. Adding a split vertex $q$ is read by coefficient $1$ of $t(\operatorname{Fr}_q)H_U$; adding a simple vertex is read on the opposite $B_U$.* 2. *Let $p\in U$ and $g=\operatorname{Fr}_q$. Changing one oriented ramification bit at $p$ changes the symbol by $$\label{co:contraction} A\bigl((U\setminus\{p\})\cup\{q'\}\bigr),\qquad \operatorname{Fr}_{q'}\sim g\phi_p,$$ unless $p,q$ are both simple, in which case the change is zero. The replacement has the product labels at all retained places. Its coefficient system has support $S\cup(U\setminus\{p\})$: the entire rational prime $p$, including both places above it when it splits in $F$, is omitted.* 3. *A split vertex with trivial label in the elementary extension for the other vertices has zero symbol.* 4. *In matching parity, insertion of two simple primes with equal rational labels on the fixed support and at the existing split vertices preserves the symbol if $\#E(\mathbb F_q)/2$ is odd for that label. Their mutual simple bits and their incidences with other simple vertices do not affect this statement.* *These are coefficient identities. Simultaneous realization of their labels by primes is a separate arithmetic construction.
  - Explanation: Symbols factor through elementary extensions, satisfy a contraction rule that omits an entire rational prime, and obey the isolation and twin-insertion rules.
- **claim-finite-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-gr-realization)): Fix finitely many old vertices, with all their mutual data, and individually realizable new types. Every prescription of the independent forward bits involving at least one new vertex has a realization by distinct fresh primes, avoiding an arbitrary finite set. The old vertices and their mutual data remain unchanged. The same holds with further fixed local tests included in $S$. A contracted split cluster may be represented by the product of its oriented generators, with conjugations prescribed along its contraction tree; its support types and external residue bits are the corresponding sums. A cluster containing one simple vertex has the corresponding rational product data.* *For a symbol satisfying the omitted-vertex dependence in Proposition <a href="#co:symbol-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:symbol-interface">54</a>, its value depends only on the graph data. Products in the preceding assertion may therefore be replaced by fresh prime representatives without changing the prescribed contracted symbol.
  - Explanation: Prescribed residue bits for new vertices are realized by fresh primes via ray class Chebotarev, leaving old data unchanged and allowing product labels.
- **claim-address-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gr-address)): Suppose the binary symbols under consideration depend only on the arithmetic graph data and satisfy the contraction, whole-rational-prime omission, and primary-isolation rules of Proposition <a href="#co:symbol-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:symbol-interface">54</a>, with the realization freedoms of Lemma <a href="#gr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:realization">55</a>. Contraction includes the product labels and external incidences, and the zero rule for two simple vertices. These identities are required only on nonempty configurations and for contractions with nonempty output. Fix $b\ge1$ and either of the following terminal arrangements.* 1. *One symbol, a nonempty set of always active terminals, and a nonzero polynomial in their independent pair bits.* 2. *One or two symbols on terminals with nonzero linear activations, including at least one terminal for every nonzero linear form. At each $x\ne0$ each symbol separately is a nonzero polynomial in the active terminal bits. The symbols may read different compatible projections of a common graph alphabet and may have fixed stationary external data.* *Suppose also that at each nonzero address the total active terminal data, together with any stationary external data, obey the required filter. Starting from any fixed realization of the terminal and stationary vertices, one can adjoin finitely many fresh primary auxiliary vertices, activated by nonzero linear forms, and choose only incidences involving these new vertices so that every symbol equals $1$ at every $x\ne0$. All data on the original vertices remain unchanged. No auxiliary is present at $0$. All auxiliaries may satisfy the fixed splitting and neutral external-incidence conditions incorporated into the primary type.* *It is enough that a terminal polynomial have a nonzero evaluation or a nonzero iterated edge difference. In particular a partitionability certificate suffices for the stationary hypothesis.
  - Explanation: Adjoining primary auxiliary primes activated by nonzero linear forms makes one or two symbols equal one at every nonzero address and leaves the origin untouched.
- **claim-oriented-forests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gr-forest)): Assume the graph-symbol rules stated in Theorem <a href="#gr:address" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:address">57</a>. Differentiation in a tree of graph bits contracts the tree, adding product labels and external incidences; its value is zero if the tree contains two simple vertices. Differentiation in any collection of edge variables containing a cycle is zero, even when different edges use incompatible relative orientations. Two different oriented variables on the same pair count as a cycle of length two.
  - Explanation: Differentiation along trees contracts them with product labels, while differentiation along any cycle vanishes.
- **claim-network-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gr-network)): A network acts on $P$ by a sum of finite-difference operators. A surviving term is a forest in which every component containing a primary vertex meets a terminal. A component meeting $t$ terminals acts by a contraction of degree $t-1$; a component containing two simple terminals is zero. Networks on disjoint sets of internal vertices, with no edges between those sets, multiply as operators on the same terminal polynomial. Consequently an operator of degree greater than $\deg P$ annihilates $P$.* *Let $Q$ be the grounded Laplacian on the internal vertices: $Q_{ij}$ for $i\ne j$ records the internal edge $ij$, while $Q_{ii}$ is the total incidence at $i$, including its terminal edges. Let $(c_a)_i$ record the incidence from internal vertex $i$ to terminal $a$. All entries are in $\mathbf F_2$. The constant and pair-contraction coefficients of the operator are respectively $$\label{gr:forest-coefficients} \det Q,\qquad c_a^{\mathsf t}\operatorname{adj}(Q)c_b\quad(a\ne b).$$ The second coefficient multiplies the terminal difference in the alignment selected by the network. A prohibited pair multiplies the zero operator.
  - Explanation: A network of primary vertices acts as a sum of finite-difference operators whose coefficients are given by Laplacian determinants and adjugate pairings.
- **claim-address-programs** (construction, [source](#stmt-gr-programs)): With the preceding grounding convention one has the following programs for every $b\ge1$.* 1. *A network $\mathcal S$ with $$\mathcal S_{x_*}=\Delta_z+O_{\ge2},\qquad \mathcal S_x=1+O_{\ge1}\quad(x\ne x_*).$$* 2. *Two networks on the same activated sites, indexed by $\varepsilon\in\mathbf F_2$, with $$\mathcal N_x^{(\varepsilon)}=1+O_{\ge1},\qquad \mathcal N_x^{(1)}+\mathcal N_x^{(0)} =\begin{cases}\Delta_z+O_{\ge2},&x=x_*,\\ O_{\ge2},&x\ne x_*. \end{cases}$$* *All nonzero-activation sites are absent at $0$. Unspecified incidences, including those to stationary external vertices, may remain neutral.
  - Explanation: Explicit grounded networks select a derivative at one target address while acting as identity plus higher-order terms elsewhere.
- **claim-collected-interfaces** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pw-interfaces)): The following statements hold for the fixed curve and fixed local data.* 1. *The cyclotomic missing-vertex statement of Theorem <a href="#cy:missing-vertex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cy:missing-vertex">5</a> applies to any fixed base $h_0$ of Selmer corank zero. If arbitrarily large binary cubes based at $h_0$, with no old prime removed, have nonzero central values at every other address and satisfy $$v(L_*(h_x))-2w(h_x)-s(h_x)\le B\quad(x\ne0),$$ then the base central value is nonzero and satisfies the same inequality with $B$ replaced by $B+C$. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed curve and local data, not on $h_0$, its number of prime factors, or the cube dimension. The dimension required for the argument may depend on the base. The cyclotomic lower bound at an analytically nonvanishing parameter is also available.* 2. *Corollary <a href="#rd:ring-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:ring-lower">11</a> has the following uniform form. If $k<0$ is an odd fundamental discriminant with at most $A$ prime factors, $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$ is neither an extra-unit field nor the CM field, $k$ is a square at $2Nh$, and the twists $h,hk$ have respective analytic orders one and zero, then their ring-class Heegner sum has free-line index $j_h(k)$ satisfying $$\label{pw:ring-lower-interface} 2j_h(k)\ge 4w(h)+s(h)+s(hk)-C_{E,A}.$$ For a fixed such $k$, Theorem <a href="#rd:missing-vertex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:missing-vertex">12</a> applies to cubes based at $h=1$ whose new primes all split in $K$. If $s_2(E/K)=1$ and the nonzero addresses have product analytic order one and uniformly bounded $2j_h(k)-4w(h)-s(h)-s(hk)$, the base product has analytic order one.* 3. *The even coefficient system, exact same-filter square comparison, divided operators, and trace identities of Proposition <a href="#co:even" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:even">46</a> and Lemmas <a href="#co:operators" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:operators">51</a>, <a href="#co:traces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:traces">52</a>, and <a href="#co:inertia" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:inertia">53</a> are available. After a uniform lower bound for the relevant point indices has been proved, the moving odd coefficient system and its stabilized minimum are available as in Proposition <a href="#co:odd" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:odd">49</a> and Lemma <a href="#co:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:minimum">50</a>. The resulting unit symbols satisfy the contraction, good-twin, and primary-isolation rules of Proposition <a href="#co:symbol-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:symbol-interface">54</a>. An even unit gives $$\label{pw:even-unit-interface} L_*(h)\ne0,\qquad v(L_*(h))\le 2w(h)+C;$$ an odd unit gives analytic order one and $$\label{pw:odd-unit-interface} j(P_h)\le w(h)+C.$$ The constants are independent of the number and sizes of the variable primes. The fixed genus partner used to define $P_h$ is denoted $h_*$.* 4. *Arithmetic realization and the forest and address statements of Lemmas <a href="#gr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:realization">55</a> and <a href="#gr:forest" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:forest">58</a> and Theorem <a href="#gr:address" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:address">57</a> hold. In particular, separate nonzero terminal polynomials may be addressed simultaneously. Primary auxiliaries preserve all fixed local classes, have neutral incidence to specified stationary external vertices, and may be required to split in any compatible fixed fields. They contribute no prime at address zero.
  - Explanation: Collects the cyclotomic transfer, ring-class lower bound, coefficient systems with unit symbols, and realization statements in the form used for assembly.
- **claim-isogeny-orientation-coranks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-isogeny-corank)): Recall that a rational isogeny preserves analytic rank, Mordell–Weil rank, and $2^\infty$-Selmer corank. If $k$ is a quadratic discriminant and $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$, then $$\label{pw:quadratic-corank} s_2(E/K)=s_2(E/\mathbb Q)+s_2(E^{(k)}/\mathbb Q).$$ For every $E/\mathbb Q$ one has, separately, $$\label{pw:kummer-corank} s_2(E)=r(E)+ \operatorname{corank}_{\mathbb Z_2}\operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty].$$
  - Explanation: Isogenies preserve the ranks, quadratic base change adds coranks, and the Kummer identity holds separately.
- **claim-minimum-seeds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-minimum-seeds)): 1. *In an all-split alphabet, suppose a unit symbol has a nonempty witness. At the least positive vertex count $r$ of such a witness, all mutual edge bits are immaterial. The symbol is determined by the individual fixed-support labels. This remains true if the empty configuration is a unit, provided no contraction to the empty configuration is used.* 2. *In the quadratic case, suppose the symbol has a nonzero witness, the alphabet is closed under the allowed contractions, and it contains the good twin operations. There are $r\ge0$ and $s_1\ge1$ such that for every $s\ge s_1$ of the permitted simple parity, the minimum split count of a unit with $s$ simple vertices is $r$. At these counts all edge bits are immaterial. A chosen minimum seed may be padded by arbitrarily many good twin pairs.
  - Explanation: At minimal witness size the mutual edge bits are immaterial and the symbol depends only on fixed-support labels, with stabilization in the quadratic case.
- **claim-finite-label-reserves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-reserves)): Fix a minimum seed and a finite label alphabet.* 1. *In the all-split rational alphabet, a list with the same total label as the seed and sufficiently many copies of its labels is partitionable.* 2. *In the quadratic alphabet, retain $r$ split anchors with the seed labels, and suppose the list and seed have the same total rational label on the projection used by the symbol. Suppose there are sufficiently many simple vertices of the required parity and sufficiently many split reserves of each realizable rational shift. If $F\ne F'$, also suppose $$\label{pw:donor-balance} \Delta=\#\{p:\ p\text{ split in }F\text{ and inert in }F'\} -\#\{p:\ p\text{ inert in }F\text{ and split in }F'\}>C_0.$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on the anchors and the reserves. Then all other split vertices can be attached to simple vertices so that the result is the minimum seed and good twin pairs. The twins may match a prescribed finite enrichment of the rational labels. The base simple labels may be specified on any projection on which the required total agrees.* *All reserve requirements depend only on the finite alphabets, seed, and projection. They do not depend on the original list length.
  - Explanation: With enough split and simple reserves and donor surplus, any large list is partitionable into a minimum seed plus good twin pairs.
- **claim-neutral-packets** (construction, [source](#stmt-pw-packets)): For any of the finite reserve problems above with prescribed rational total zero, one can choose a finite packet of terminal labels with rational total zero such that every nonempty union of copies of the packet has the required supplies. In the quadratic case with $F\ne F'$, its donor surplus may be made arbitrarily large. When $F=F'$, every simple label is good and no donor surplus is required. Compatible fixed splitting conditions may be included whenever the needed individual labels and shifts remain realizable.
  - Explanation: A finite packet of labels with zero rational total supplies the required reserves for every nonempty union of copies.
- **claim-partitionable-even-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-pw-partitionable-even)): Fix an even symbol furnished by Proposition <a href="#pw:interfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:interfaces">61</a>. For every partitionable parameter $h$ on its filter with $s_2(E^{(h)})=0$, $$\label{pw:partitionable-bound} L_*(h)\ne0,\qquad v(L_*(h))-2w(h)-s(h)\le C.$$ The constant depends only on the symbol and fixed local data.
  - Explanation: Every partitionable parameter at corank zero has nonzero central value and bounded defect valuation.
- **claim-even-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-even-base)): If $s_2(E)=0$, then $a(E)=0$.
  - Explanation: Selmer corank zero for the original curve forces analytic rank zero via addressing and cyclotomic transfer.
- **claim-bounded-companion** (construction, [source](#stmt-pw-rough-companion)): Fix a positive odd-sign filter, including the coefficient and class-group support used below, and a negative genus partner $h_*$ with $L(E^{(h_*)},1)\ne0$, chosen using Proposition <a href="#nv:local-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:local-witness">43</a> and then excluded from the variable parameters. There are constants $A,C$ such that every $h$ on this filter of analytic order one admits a negative odd fundamental discriminant $k'$ for which* 1. *$(k',2Nh)=1$, $k'$ is a square at every place of $2Nh$, and $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k'})$ is nonexceptional;* 2. *$\omega(k')\le A$;* 3. *$L(E^{(hk')},1)\ne0$ and $$\label{pw:rough-bound} v(L_*(hk'))\le 2w(hk')+s(hk')+C.$$
  - Explanation: For each analytic rank one parameter there is a negative companion discriminant with boundedly many prime factors whose twist has nonzero central value and a bounded defect.
- **claim-height-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-pw-height-comparison)): Here $j_h(k)$ is the index of the conductor-$\|h\|$ ring-class sum over $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$, whereas $j(P_h)$ is the index of the conductor-one genus sum over $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{hh_*})$. With the fixed modular parametrization and the fixed genus partner $h_*$, the explicit Gross–Zagier formula gives, whenever the relevant factors have analytic orders one and zero, $$\label{pw:height-ratio} 2j_h(k)-2j(P_h)=v(L_*(hk))+O(1).$$ For $k$ with bounded prime count as in Lemma <a href="#pw:rough-companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:rough-companion">68</a>, the error is uniform in $h,k$; for a fixed $k$ it is uniform in $h$.
  - Explanation: The explicit Gross-Zagier formula compares the ring-class and genus Heegner indices through the valuation of the twisted central value up to a bounded error.
- **claim-odd-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-pw-odd-lower)): Every analytically rank-one $h$ in the prepared positive filter of Lemma <a href="#pw:rough-companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:rough-companion">68</a> satisfies $$\label{pw:odd-lower-bound} 2j(P_h)\ge 2w(h)+s(h)-C.$$ In particular $j(P_h)\ge w(h)-C$ uniformly in $h$ and its prime count.
  - Explanation: Every analytically rank one parameter in the prepared filter has Heegner index bounded below in terms of weight and Shafarevich-Tate length.
- **claim-recurrence-congruences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-recurrence)): Let $p$ be odd, $a\equiv p+1\pmod4$, and let $$b_0=1,\quad b_1=a-\kappa,\quad b_{s+1}=ab_s-pb_{s-1}.$$ If $\kappa=\pm1$, then $b_{s+1}-b_s$ is even and $$\frac{b_{s+1}-b_s}{2}\equiv\frac{a-\kappa-1}{2}\pmod2 \quad(s\ge0).$$ If $\kappa=0$, put $b_{-1}=0$; then $b_s-b_{s-1}$ is odd for every $s\ge0$. Finally, for $\kappa=1$, if $a\equiv p+1\pmod{2^m}$ then $b_s\equiv p^s\pmod{2^m}$ for every $s\ge0$.
  - Explanation: A second order recurrence satisfies parity and divided-difference congruences determined by the trace and the character value.
- **claim-split-witness** (construction, [source](#stmt-pw-split-witness)): Suppose $E[2]$ is rational. Choose a negative unit $k$ of a fixed even symbol for $E$, prime to all previous fixed support and to the ramification support of $E[8]$. Put $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$, chosen nonexceptional. Let $S_0$ contain the complete fixed support of the coefficient system, $2N$, the primes of $k$, and primes generating the class group of $K$. On the positive filter of $1$ square at $S_0$, there is an even unit test on nonempty products of primes split in $K$ with these properties:* 1. *it has a nonempty witness;* 2. *it satisfies the oriented split contraction and primary-isolation rules over $K$, for contractions with nonempty output;* 3. *a unit at $D$ implies $$\label{pw:split-unit-bound} L(E^{(kD)},1)\ne0,\qquad v(L_*(kD))\le 2\omega(D)+C_k.$$* *This conclusion uses the integral trace and local identities in Proposition <a href="#pw:interfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:interfaces">61</a>(3).
  - Explanation: With full rational two-torsion there is an even unit test on nonempty products of primes split in the companion field with a nonempty witness and bounded valuation.
- **claim-orientation-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-coinvariants)): Let $K$ be imaginary quadratic, let $S_0$ contain $2$ and its ramification support, and let $V_0$ be the elementary label space over $K$ unramified off $S_0$. Complex conjugation acts on $V_0$ by $c$. If the rational total of an oriented prime configuration is zero on every rational quadratic character unramified off $S_0$, then its oriented total belongs to $(1+c)V_0$. A reserved supply containing every label allows its orientations to be flipped to realize any prescribed change in $(1+c)V_0$.
  - Explanation: Zero rational total makes the oriented total lie in the image of one plus conjugation, so orientation flips realize any such correction.
- **claim-odd-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pw-odd-base)): If $s_2(E)=1$, then $a(E)=1$.
  - Explanation: Selmer corank one for the original curve forces analytic rank one via simultaneous odd and even unit vertices and ring-class transfer.

        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, depending only on the fixed curve and local classes, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rl-finite-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(A,\\mathfrak m,k)$ be an Artin local ring, and let $D$ be a complex of flat $A$-modules. Suppose $H^*(D\\otimes_A k)$ is finite dimensional and supported in $[a,b]$. There is a bounded finite free complex $C$, with $$\\operatorname{rank}_A C^j=\\dim_k H^j(D\\otimes_A k),$$ and a strong deformation retraction $$i:C\\longrightarrow D,\\qquad p:D\\longrightarrow C,\\qquad\n pi=1,\\qquad 1-ip=dh+hd.$$ Thus maps to the original complex, as well as maps between finite models, are available. Any fixed finite collection of chain maps, homotopies, triangles, and perfect pairings can be transported to these models.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rl-finite-model",

Any fixed finite collection of chain maps, homotopies, triangles, and perfect pairings can be transported to these models.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-rl-finite-limits",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose diagrams over $A_i$, or over $A_i[G]$, have uniformly bounded cohomological amplitudes and graded free ranks, and include the chain homotopies and inverses for every asserted identity or equivalence. After taking a cofinal subsequence, or a nonprincipal ultrafilter limit, their matrices define the same diagram of perfect complexes over $\\Lambda$, or $\\Lambda[G]$.*\n\n*Moreover, the contractions into the original cochains may be retained for the evaluation construction in Lemma <a href=\"#rl:evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:evaluation\">19</a>.         "source_id": "PM-004.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rl-kummer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a finite extension of a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, and let $B/F$ be any elliptic curve. Put $M_F=B(F)^\\wedge_2=\\varprojlim_n B(F)/2^nB(F)$. There is a perfect local condition $$U_F=M_F[-1]\\longrightarrow R\\Gamma(F,T_2B).$$ For every $n\\geq1$, derived reduction has $$H^0(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)[2^n],\\qquad\n H^1(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)/2^nB(F).$$ Its map in degree zero is the isomorphism onto $H^0(F,B[2^n])$, and its map in degree one is the finite Kummer injection. The condition is its own exact orthogonal complement under local Tate duality. These assertions hold at $F\\mid2$, for all reduction types, and without a condition on $B[2]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rl-kummer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a finite extension of a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, and let $B/F$ be any elliptic curve. Put $M_F=B(F)^\\wedge_2=\\varprojlim_n B(F)/2^nB(F)$. There is a perfect local condition $$U_F=M_F[-1]\\longrightarrow R\\Gamma(F,T_2B).$$ For every $n\\geq1$, derived reduction has $$H^0(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)[2^n],\\qquad\n H^1(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)/2^nB(F).$$ Its map in degree zero is the isomorphism onto $H^0(F,B[2^n])$, and its map in degree one is the finite Kummer injection. The condition is its own exact orthogonal complement under local Tate duality. These assertions hold at $F\\mid2$, for all reduction types, and without a condition on $B[2]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be a finite extension of a nonarchimedean local field of characteristic zero, and let $B/F$ be any elliptic curve. There is a perfect local condition $$U_F=M_F[-1]\\longrightarrow R\\Gamma(F,T_2B).$$ For every $n\\geq1$, derived reduction has $$H^0(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)[2^n],\\qquad\n H^1(U_F\\otimes^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbf Z/2^n)=B(F)/2^nB(F).$$ Its map in degree zero is the isomorphism onto $H^0(F,B[2^n])$, and its map in degree one is the finite Kummer injection.         "anchor": "stmt-rl-characters",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many split primes of $K$ above odd rational primes, outside $2N$, and orient one prime in each conjugate pair. We can require $r_i\\to1$ in $\\mathbf Z_2$ and $$\\label{rl:trace-bound}\n v_2(2+a_{r_i}(E)),\\quad v_2(2-a_{r_i}(E)),\\quad\n v_2\\det(1\\pm\\operatorname{Fr}_{r_i}\\mid T)\\ \\leq c_E.$$ The constant $c_E$ is independent of the list and of $K$.",

        "exact_quote": "For the individual coefficient system $T\\chi_h\\Psi$, use full local conditions at the moving prime $r$ and the active primes dividing $h$, without adding primes from a larger binary family.       "id": "claim-local-height-one-tests",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rl-local-acyclic",
          "exact_quote": "For the individual coefficient system $T\\chi_h\\Psi$, use full local conditions at the moving prime $r$ and the active primes dividing $h$, without adding primes from a larger binary family. The limiting full local complexes are acyclic over every height-one residue field of $\\Lambda$ of characteristic zero. At $(2)$, with residue field $\\Omega=\\mathbf F_2((t))$, the $r$-complex is acyclic, and so are the complexes at active split primes whose prescribed exponent is odd. These full conditions therefore agree with their orthogonal conditions on the stated tests.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rl-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ and a sequence of the finite cochain constructions above. Keep the contraction maps into the original global cochains. Let $\\Gamma_K=\\prod_i G_K$ and $\\Gamma=\\Gamma_K\\rtimes\\langle\\tau\\rangle\\subset\\prod_iG_{\\mathbf Q}$, with simultaneous complex conjugation $\\tau$. Thus $\\Gamma_K$ has index two in $\\Gamma$; the larger unrestricted product of rational Galois groups is not used for induction. The matrix limit defines a coefficient action of $\\Gamma_K$ on $\\Lambda^2$. For every field $L$ receiving a map from $\\Lambda$, there is a natural injection $$H^1(C\\otimes_\\Lambda L)\\longrightarrow\n H^1_{\\rm abs}(\\Gamma_K,L^2),$$ where the right side denotes abstract crossed-homomorphism classes. Degree-zero evaluation likewise injects $H^0(C\\otimes_\\Lambda L)$ into $(L^2)^{\\Gamma_K}$. Here $C$ may be the global complex or a Selmer complex whose local condition maps induce isomorphisms on local $H^0$ and injections on local $H^1$. The same statement holds for the induced representation of $\\Gamma$.*\n\n*There are evaluation maps on arbitrary sequences, not just on the finite list used to detect cohomology. Their degree-two identities remain valid after arbitrary coefficient extension.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rl-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "In the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#rl:evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:evaluation\">19</a>, let $H$ be the group of sequences each of whose entries acts trivially on the full Tate module, on all ring-class fields of $K$, and on the quadratic characters in use. Restriction from the crossed classes in that Lemma to $H$ is injective at every characteristic-zero test. It is also injective at the residual $(2)$ test.         "anchor": "stmt-rl-outer-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C_j$ be any family of the preceding limiting complexes, completed over $\\mathcal O_j=\\widehat{\\Lambda_{(2)}}$, possibly for different quadratic fields $K_j$. Suppose their minimal ranks are bounded. Keep their actual evaluation matrices, and transport them through the integral contractions to these minimal models. For a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\\mathcal U$, put $$\\mathcal O_\\infty=\n \\left(\\prod_{\\mathcal U}\\mathcal O_j\\right)/\\mathfrak a,\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak a=\\{(x_j):\n    \\{j:v_2(x_j)\\geq n\\}\\in\\mathcal U\\text{ for every }n\\}.$$ Then $\\mathcal O_\\infty$ is a DVR with uniformizer $2$ and a characteristic-zero fraction field $L_\\infty$. The outer complex has an injection of its $H^1$ over $L_\\infty$ into crossed classes on the product of the inner sequence groups. Its restriction to the product of their exact common kernels is injective. All arbitrary-sequence degree-two evaluation identities survive. These conclusions also hold after retaining any fixed finite collection of additional integral diagrams.",

        "exact_quote": "For any element $u$ of an exact kernel in Lemma <a href=\"#rl:kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:kernel\">20</a>, derivative primes can be chosen, at increasing finite precision, whose rational Frobenius is $\\tau u$ on all retained data.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rl-limit-evaluation-package",
        "exact_quote": "For the ring data fixed at the start of this section, retain the finite target-side arithmetic diagrams with their maps to original cochains, homotopies, and dualities as in Lemma <a href=\"#rl:finite-limits\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:finite-limits\">14</a>. The moving characters, local conditions, and evaluation constructions then have the following simultaneous properties:*\n\n1.  *Every specified finite target-side arithmetic diagram admits a bounded free model together with maps to its original cochains; the specified maps, homotopies and dualities survive the inner limit.*\n\n2.  *At the stated height-one tests the relaxed active local complexes are acyclic, and the Selmer duality pairs degrees one and two. Integral Kummer conditions at $2N$ include their full derived degree-zero invariants.*\n\n3.  *The residual sizes needed for the outer construction are bounded independently of the active conductor support, with dependence only on $E$, the bound on $\\omega(\\operatorname{disc}K)$, and the number of retained derivative primes.*\n\n4.  *Inner and outer $H^1$ are detected by actual crossed cocycles, including on the exact common kernel. The evaluations and their degree-two identities can be realized by finite Chebotarev conditions without disturbing earlier finite data.*\n\n*These assertions do not assert an Euler-system reciprocity law or an integral derivative class that has not first been constructed at finite level. Once those finite arithmetic diagrams are constructed, this Theorem supplies their limit compatibility.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

The moving characters, local conditions, and evaluation constructions then have the following simultaneous properties:*\n\n1.  *Every specified finite target-side arithmetic diagram admits a bounded free model together with maps to its original cochains; the specified maps, homotopies and dualities survive the inner limit.*\n\n2.  *At the stated height-one tests the relaxed active local complexes are acyclic, and the Selmer duality pairs degrees one and two. Integral Kummer conditions at $2N$ include their full derived degree-zero invariants.*\n\n3.  *The residual sizes needed for the outer construction are bounded independently of the active conductor support, with dependence only on $E$, the bound on $\\omega(\\operatorname{disc}K)$, and the number of retained derivative primes.*\n\n4.  *Inner and outer $H^1$ are detected by actual crossed cocycles, including on the exact common kernel.
