```json
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-local-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let a sequence of inert good primes $\\ell$ have rational Frobenius lifts tending on $T$ to a fixed complex conjugation $J$. At the unique prime of $K$ above $\\ell$, the limiting local complex has $$H^0=M,\\qquad H^1=F\\oplus S=M\\oplus M,\\qquad H^2=M,\n \\label{rd:local-cohomology}$$ with zero differentials in a free split model. Here $M=T\\otimes\\Lambda$ with the relevant scalar coefficient structure. The two degree-one coordinates of a cocycle are $$f(c)=c(\\gamma^2),\\qquad s(c)=c(\\sigma),\n \\label{rd:coordinates}$$ where $\\gamma$ is the chosen rational Frobenius lift and $\\sigma$ is an oriented pro-$2$ tame generator. The plane conditions $F=\\{s=0\\}$ and $S=\\{f=0\\}$ include $H^0$ and exclude $H^2$. Each is its own orthogonal complement for conjugate-Weil duality. The strict condition $H^0$ and the relaxed condition $H^0\\oplus H^1[-1]$ are mutually dual. All four inclusions can be equipped with compatible integral duality triangles.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Here $M=T\\otimes\\Lambda$ with the relevant scalar coefficient structure. The plane conditions $F=\\{s=0\\}$ and $S=\\{f=0\\}$ include $H^0$ and exclude $H^2$. Each is its own orthogonal complement for conjugate-Weil duality.           "exact_quote": "Let a sequence of inert good primes $\\ell$ have rational Frobenius lifts tending on $T$ to a fixed complex conjugation $J$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-derivative",
        "exact_quote": "Perform each finite ring-class construction and its weighted transfer before retaining its target-side data by the construction following Lemma <a href=\"#rl:finite-limits\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:finite-limits\">14</a>. There is one integer $L\\ne0$, depending on the fixed curve, parametrization and fixed local types, such that the classes obtained from the Heegner derivatives of $L$ times the point satisfy, for every finite switched set $I$, $$P_I\\in H^1(C_I),\\qquad\n f(P_{I\\cup\\{\\ell\\}})=0,\\qquad\n s(P_{I\\cup\\{\\ell\\}})=J f(P_I).\n \\label{rd:switch}$$ The same $L$ works for every number of active and derivative primes. The previously imposed singular conditions are retained.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-det-switch",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O$ be a DVR with fraction field $F_O$. Suppose that integral perfect complexes $C_{\\rm str},C_F,C_S,C_{\\rm rel}$, their localization triangles, and their dualities are the base changes of the diagram in Lemma <a href=\"#rd:local-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:local-model\">25</a>. Suppose the generic cohomology of these four global complexes is concentrated in degrees one and two, that $H^1(C_F\\otimes F_O)=F_OA$, and $$f(A)=v_0\\ne0,\\qquad s(B)=Jv_0,\\qquad f(B)=0$$ for a class $B$ in the switched complex. The determinant tensors are formed from each class and its functional under these same dualities. Then the switched generic cohomology has rank one, is spanned by $B$, and $$v_O(u_F)=v_O(u_S).\n \\label{rd:valuation-switch}$$ Neither localization is required to be primitive.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-rank-two",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal G$ be a group, $H$ a normal subgroup acting trivially on an absolutely irreducible representation $W$ over a field $F_O$, and suppose that restriction of the relevant cohomology classes to $H$ is injective. For two independent classes, their joint evaluations on $H$ span $W\\oplus W$. If the finite localization is the rank-two projection supplied by $1+\\tau$ on the induced representation, some actual evaluation has rank two. One of the two classes may be any prescribed nonzero class.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-rd-minor",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$C_i=[\\,O_i^n\\xrightarrow{D_i}O_i^n\\,]$$ be based complexes over DVRs with uniformizer $2$, with generic rank-one kernel. Let $z_i\\in\\ker D_i$ be integral, and let $\\lambda_i\\in 2^{-a}(O_i^n)^*$ annihilate $\\operatorname{im}D_i$, where $a$ is fixed. If an $(n-1)$-minor $\\Delta_i$ has $v(\\Delta_i)\\le b$, the determinant coordinate $u_i$ of $z_i\\otimes\\lambda_i$ satisfies $$v(u_i)\\ge-a-b.\n \\label{rd:minor-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-rd-outer-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Assume all of the following properties of a collection of primitive ring instances:*\n\n1.  *after any fixed number of switches, minimal $\\mathcal O$-models have size bounded independently of $h$ and of $K$ with $\\omega(k)\\le A$;*\n\n2.  *the all-sequence evaluation and exact-kernel restriction of Proposition <a href=\"#rl:outer-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:outer-evaluation\">21</a>, and the finite Chebotarev realization of Lemma <a href=\"#rl:chebotarev-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:chebotarev-evaluation\">23</a>, hold for these models, retaining their degree-two cocycle identities;*\n\n3.  *the integral localization and duality diagrams and the derivative classes of Lemma <a href=\"#rd:derivative\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:derivative\">26</a> survive this same outer limit; the class and its closed dual functional have a common bounded clearing exponent.*\n\n*Then there is $C(E,A)$ such that $$v_{(2)}(u)\\ge-C(E,A)\n \\label{rd:residual-bound}$$ for every simple-zero ring instance.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-rd-outer-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Assume all of the following properties of a collection of primitive ring instances:*\n\n1.  *after any fixed number of switches, minimal $\\mathcal O$-models have size bounded independently of $h$ and of $K$ with $\\omega(k)\\le A$;*\n\n2.  *the all-sequence evaluation and exact-kernel restriction of Proposition <a href=\"#rl:outer-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:outer-evaluation\">21</a>, and the finite Chebotarev realization of Lemma <a href=\"#rl:chebotarev-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:chebotarev-evaluation\">23</a>, hold for these models, retaining their degree-two cocycle identities;*\n\n3.  *the integral localization and duality diagrams and the derivative classes of Lemma <a href=\"#rd:derivative\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:derivative\">26</a> survive this same outer limit; the class and its closed dual functional have a common bounded clearing exponent.*\n\n*Then there is $C(E,A)$ such that $$v_{(2)}(u)\\ge-C(E,A)\n \\label{rd:residual-bound}$$ for every simple-zero ring instance. The hypotheses refer to a single simultaneous construction. In particular, separate abstract models realizing its individual cohomology groups do not establish this proposition’s arithmetic applicability.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-rd-outer-bound"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-derived-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a perfect $\\Lambda$-complex and put $C_0=C\\otimes_\\Lambda^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbb Z_2$, where $t=0$. Suppose its generic rational cohomology and its central rational cohomology both have dimension one in degrees one and two and are zero in other degrees. Put $A=\\Lambda_{(t)}$, with residue field $\\mathbb Q_2$. Let $z$ be a degree-one cycle and $\\lambda$ a closed degree-two functional in a finite free model of $C\\otimes_\\Lambda A$, and let $z_0,\\lambda_0$ be their images under $A\\to\\mathbb Q_2$. Then the generic determinant tensor $[z]\\otimes[\\lambda]$ is regular at $(t)$ and specializes, under the determinant base-change isomorphism, to $[z_0]\\otimes[\\lambda_0]$. Its central valuation is computed from the entire integral complex $C_0$, including its torsion cohomology. It cannot be computed by substituting the naive quotient of $H^1(C)$ by $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Its central valuation is computed from the entire integral complex $C_0$, including its torsion cohomology. It cannot be computed by substituting the naive quotient of $H^1(C)$ by $t$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-active-local",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_v/\\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified, $p\\ne2$, and let $T'=T_2(E)\\otimes\\chi$, where $E$ has good reduction and $\\chi$ is ramified quadratic.         "anchor": "stmt-rd-central-formula",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the product $$L(E^{(h)},s)L(E^{(hk)},s)$$ has a simple zero at $s=1$. Use the primitive Heegner sum $P_h$ and its free-line index $j_h$ defined at the start of Section <a href=\"#rl:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rl:section\">4</a>, and put $$s(h)=\\operatorname{length}_{\\mathbb Z_2}\n             \\operatorname{Sha}(E^{(h)}/\\mathbb Q)[2^\\infty].$$ Use the primitive Selmer determinant coordinate $u_h$ with the fixed class multiplier of Lemma <a href=\"#rd:derivative\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:derivative\">26</a>. For the primitive Selmer diagrams constructed above, we have $$\\begin{split}\n v(u_h(0))\n  &=2j_h-\n    \\operatorname{length}_{\\mathbb Z_2}\n                  \\operatorname{Sha}(E^{(h)}/K)[2^\\infty]\n    -2\\sum_{p\\mid h}t_p+O_E(1)\\\\\n  &=2j_h-s(h)-s(hk)-4w(h)+O_{E,A}(1),\n       \\qquad \\omega(k)\\le A .\n \\end{split}\n \\label{rd:central-value}$$ The first error includes the auxiliary prime and the fixed parametrization multiplier. The second has no dependence on the number of primes dividing $h$.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-rd-schur",
        "exact_quote": "There is a natural identification $$\\mathcal O/2^M\\mathcal O\n       =(\\mathbb Z/2^M\\mathbb Z)((t)).\n \\label{rd:laurent}$$ Suppose $A=\\operatorname{diag}(A_1,\\ldots,A_m)$ has entries in $\\Lambda$, each residual determinant is nonzero, and $\\operatorname{ord}_t(\\det\\overline A_j)\\le h$. If $B=A+2E$ with $E$ over $\\Lambda$, then every entry of $B^{-1}\\bmod2^M$ has no term below $t^{-Mh}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-clearing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a based complex of finite free $\\mathcal O[G]$-modules of total graded rank at most $r_0$, with $D_0$ defined over $\\Lambda$. Suppose $D_0\\otimes_\\Lambda^{\\mathbf L}\\mathbb Z_2$ has rational cohomology of dimension one in degrees one and two, zero elsewhere, and total integral torsion length at most $h_1$. Let $z$ be an integral cycle and let $\\lambda\\in2^{-a_0}(D^2)^*$ be a closed functional. Assume every character constituent is either generically acyclic or has the same generic rank-one pattern; in the generically acyclic case its determinant coordinate is defined to be zero. Multiplication of these coordinates by any $\\eta\\in\\mathcal O[G]$ is permitted. Then there are $f,d\\in\\mathcal O[G]$ such that $$f_xu_x=d_x,\\qquad\n f_0\\in\\Lambda,\\qquad\n v(f_0(0))\\le C(r_0,h_1,a_0)<\\infty.\n \\label{rd:clearing-equations}$$ If all entries of $D_x$ have bounded negative Laurent support at each precision, uniformly in $b$, the same is true of $f_x$. No such bound on $d_x,z,\\lambda,\\eta$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rd-clearing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a based complex of finite free $\\mathcal O[G]$-modules of total graded rank at most $r_0$, with $D_0$ defined over $\\Lambda$.         "exact_quote": "Assume the compatible universal Selmer and Heegner diagrams and their primitive specializations.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-assembly",
        "exact_quote": "For the ring data stated at the start of this section, the actual finite Heegner and Selmer constructions satisfy the simultaneous diagram hypotheses of Propositions <a href=\"#rd:away-two\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:away-two\">29</a>, <a href=\"#rd:outer-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:outer-bound\">31</a>, and <a href=\"#rd:central-formula\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:central-formula\">34</a>. The bounds needed for the lower-bound argument depend only on $E$, the fixed local data, and a bound $A$ on $\\omega(k)$. For the binary data <a href=\"#rd:binary-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rd:binary-data\">[rd:binary-data]</a> with fixed $E,K$ and base Selmer corank one, assume in addition that every character constituent of the universal Selmer complex is generically acyclic or has generic cohomology of dimension one in degrees one and two and zero elsewhere.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rd-binary-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f,d\\in\\mathcal O[G]$, $u_x\\in\\Lambda[1/2]$, and $f_xu_x=d_x$ for every character. Suppose $f_0\\in\\Lambda$ and $c=v(f_0(0))<\\infty$. If every $f_x\\bmod2^M$ has no powers below $-L(M)$, then for $$M>c,\\qquad\n b>(L(M)+2)(2^M-1)$$ there is $x\\ne0$ satisfying $$v\\bigl(u_x(0)-u_0(0)\\bigr)\\ge M-2c.\n \\label{rd:binary-congruence}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix an elliptic curve $A/\\mathbb Q$ and a nonempty filter of fundamental discriminants prime to $2N_A$, with specified real sign and unit squareclasses at finitely many primes.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nv-moment",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\eta>0$ and $B>0$ such that the following assertions hold. For squarefree $b$ prime to $H$, put $$S_{j,b}(X)=\n\\sum_{\\substack{n>0\\ {\\rm squarefree}\\\\ n\\equiv a\\ (H)\\\\b\\mid n}}\n L^{(j)}(1/2,f\\otimes\\chi_{\\sigma n})\\Phi(n/X).$$ If the common root number is $+1$, then $$S_{0,b}(X)=cX\\prod_{p\\mid b}\\beta_p+\n O_{f,H,\\Phi}(b^B X^{1-\\eta}),\n\\qquad\nc>0,\\quad 0<\\beta_p<1,\\quad\n\\beta_p=\\frac1p+O(p^{-2}).$$ The last implied constant is absolute. If the common root number is $-1$, then $$S_{1,1}(X)=cX\\log X+c_1X+\n O_{f,H,\\Phi}(X^{1-\\eta}),\\qquad c>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nv-moment",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\eta>0$ and $B>0$ such that the following assertions hold. If the common root number is $-1$, then $$S_{1,1}(X)=cX\\log X+c_1X+\n O_{f,H,\\Phi}(X^{1-\\eta}),\\qquad c>0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-even",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an even-sign filter and a nonvanishing index $D_0$ in it. There is a holomorphic cuspidal form $g=\\sum_{n\\geq1}a(n)q^n$ of weight $3/2$, orthogonal to unary theta series, of level supported on a fixed enlargement of $S$, and characters $\\eta,\\psi$ of $2$-power order such that $\\psi/\\eta=\\chi_{-\\sigma}$ and $$\\label{co:recurrence}\n a(p^2n)=\\bigl(\\eta(p)a_p(E)-\\psi(p)(-n/p)\\bigr)a(n)\n              -p\\eta(p)^2a(n/p^2)\\qquad(p\\notin S).$$ Its coefficients are zero off the unit-squareclass filter. After normalization at $D_0$, every squarefree coefficient is rational up to a $2$-power root of unity. For two nonzero squarefree coefficients, $$\\label{co:exact-ratio}\n 2v\\!\\left(\\frac{a(D)}{a(D_0)}\\right)\n   =v\\!\\left(\\frac{L_*(\\sigma D)}{L_*(\\sigma D_0)}\\right).$$ Moreover $a(D)\\neq0$ exactly when $L(E^{(\\sigma D)},1)\\neq0$. Consequently any previously established bound $v(L_*(\\sigma D))\\geq2w(D)+s(\\sigma D)-C$ gives $$\\label{co:even-bound}\n v(a(D))\\geq w(D)+\\tfrac12s(\\sigma D)-C_g$$ with $C_g$ fixed independently of $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

After normalization at $D_0$, every squarefree coefficient is rational up to a $2$-power root of unity. For two nonzero squarefree coefficients, $$\\label{co:exact-ratio}\n 2v\\!\\left(\\frac{a(D)}{a(D_0)}\\right)\n   =v\\!\\left(\\frac{L_*(\\sigma D)}{L_*(\\sigma D_0)}\\right).$$ Moreover $a(D)\\neq0$ exactly when $L(E^{(\\sigma D)},1)\\neq0$. Consequently any previously established bound $v(L_*(\\sigma D))\\geq2w(D)+s(\\sigma D)-C$ gives $$\\label{co:even-bound}\n v(a(D))\\geq w(D)+\\tfrac12s(\\sigma D)-C_g$$ with $C_g$ fixed independently of $D$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-co-kummer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ act on $T=T_2E$ as complex conjugation, and choose a nonzero integral row $b:T\\longrightarrow\\mathbb Z_2$ with $bC=b$. For a fixed positive simple-zero witness $D_w$, there are arbitrarily large precisions $M$ and good primes $\\ell$ such that $$\\label{co:ell-conditions}\n \\rho_T(\\operatorname{Fr}_\\ell)\\equiv C\\pmod {2^M},\\qquad\n (h_*/\\ell)=-1,\\qquad(D_w/\\ell)=1.$$ Evaluation at Frobenius squared defines an integral homomorphism $$\\lambda_M:E(\\mathbb F_{\\ell^2})\\longrightarrow\\mathbb Z/2^M\\mathbb Z,\n \\qquad \\lambda_M=b\\kappa_M,$$ of Frobenius sign $+1$. The valuation of $\\lambda_M(\\operatorname{red}_\\ell(LP_{D_w}))$ is bounded independently of $M$, for a fixed nonzero integer $L$ making the twist comparison integral. Extra fixed finite-field restrictions can be retained when they agree with the chosen complex-conjugation coset, or when a compatible lift has been specified.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

The valuation of $\\lambda_M(\\operatorname{red}_\\ell(LP_{D_w}))$ is bounded independently of $M$, for a fixed nonzero integer $L$ making the twist comparison integral.           "exact_quote": "Let $C$ act on $T=T_2E$ as complex conjugation, and choose a nonzero integral row $b:T\\longrightarrow\\mathbb Z_2$ with $bC=b$.         "anchor": "stmt-co-theta-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\ell$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#co:kummer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:kummer\">47</a>, the reduced modular parametrization followed by $\\lambda_M$ admits an integral weighted ternary theta lift. After extraction of the coefficients at multiples of $|h_*|$ it gives a classical holomorphic weight-$3/2$ form $g_M=\\sum a_M(n)q^n$, of level supported on $S\\cup\\{\\ell\\}$, with <a href=\"#co:recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"co:recurrence\">[co:recurrence]</a> modulo $2^M$. The associated characters have $2$-power order and quadratic ratio. For squarefree admissible $D$, $$\\label{co:weighted-comparison}\n a_M(D)=c_0\\,2^{\\omega(N)}\\zeta_M(D)\n       \\lambda_M\\bigl(\\operatorname{red}_\\ell(LP_D)\\bigr)\n                     \\pmod {2^M},$$ where $\\zeta_M(D)$ is a $2$-power root of unity and $c_0,L$ are fixed integers independent of $D,M,\\ell$.         "anchor": "stmt-co-minimum",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the uniform arithmetic lower bounds just stated. Each coefficient source defines a binary symbol $$\\label{co:symbol}\n A(D)=\\begin{cases}\n 1,&v(a(D))=\\delta+w(D),\\\\\n 0,&v(a(D))>\\delta+w(D)\\text{ or }D\\text{ is off the filter},\n \\end{cases}$$ with a fixed minimum normalized depth $\\delta$ and at least one bounded-weight witness. For the odd source $a=a_M$ at an actual stage: the numerical minimum and its divisibility statements stabilize at every fixed finite weight bound. The finite symbol tables on arithmetic labels will be stabilized separately in Section <a href=\"#pw:odd-stages\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pw:odd-stages\">9.5</a>. At a unit symbol, $$v(L_*(\\sigma D))\\leq2w(D)+O(1)\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad j(P_D)\\leq w(D)+O(1),$$ respectively, and the analytic order is respectively zero or one.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-operators",
        "exact_quote": "For the even source let $U$ be arbitrary. For the odd source, fix an integer $R\\ge1$ and a weight bound $W$; all assertions below are made at a sufficiently advanced actual stage, simultaneously for every $U$ with $w(U)\\le W$ and every Fourier index. In these respective settings the coefficients of $B_U$ are integral. Write uniquely $n=Jrm^2$, with $J\\subseteq U$, $r$ squarefree, and $(r,U)=1$. The coefficient vanishes unless $D=(U/J)r$ belongs to the filter, and for the even source otherwise $$\\label{co:BU-bound}\n v(B_U[n])\\geq w(r)+\\tfrac12\\#\\{p\\in J:p\\text{ simple}\\}-e_U.$$ At that stage the odd source satisfies this inequality for every $U$ with $w(U)\\le W$ and every $n$, with its right side replaced by its minimum with $R$. In particular the untruncated bound holds on any prescribed bounded range of squarefree weights, after choosing $R$ and the stage. For a split $p\\in J$ the difference factor, after its unit phase and one factor of $2$ are removed, has residue $$\\label{co:cp}\n c_p(D)=\\frac{a_p(E)-(\\psi/\\eta)(p)(-D/p)-1}{2}\\pmod2$$ at every square shell. For a simple $p$ this formula holds on the first shell $p\\nmid m$.*\n\n\n*Choose $i\\in\\{0,1\\}$ such that $\\eta\\chi_{-4}^i$ is odd, and set $$\\Theta_J=\\sum_{\\substack{m>0\\\\(m,S)=1}}\n              \\eta(m)\\chi_{-4}(m)^i m q^{Jm^2}.$$ For matching $U$ the modular form $$\\label{co:HU}\n H_U=\\frac12\\left(B_U-\n       \\sum_{\\substack{J\\subseteq U\\\\J\\text{ split only}}}\n                 B_U[J]\\Theta_J\\right)$$ has integral coefficients, with the same stage and weight restriction in the odd case. For opposite $U$ put $H_U=B_U$. The new primes in the level of $\\Theta_J$ have exponent one, and its character is $\\eta\\chi_{-4}^i(-J/\\cdot)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-traces",
        "exact_quote": "The module $\\mathcal M$ is an integral Fourier lattice preserved by the trace and determinant operators. On the coefficientwise reduced trace orbit of the indicated vector, $$\\label{co:trace-identities}\n d(u)t(v)=t(v),\\qquad\n t(u)t(v)=t(uv)+t(uv^{-1}),\\qquad t(1)=0.$$ Every coefficient test of a finite product of traces can be performed using distinct fresh primes avoiding the tested index. In particular testing $t(u_1)\\cdots t(u_a)$ at $n$ becomes testing the original reduced coefficient at $nq_1\\cdots q_a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-traces",
          "exact_quote": "The module $\\mathcal M$ is an integral Fourier lattice preserved by the trace and determinant operators. On the coefficientwise reduced trace orbit of the indicated vector, $$\\label{co:trace-identities}\n d(u)t(v)=t(v),\\qquad\n t(u)t(v)=t(uv)+t(uv^{-1}),\\qquad t(1)=0.$$ Every coefficient test of a finite product of traces can be performed using distinct fresh primes avoiding the tested index. In particular testing $t(u_1)\\cdots t(u_a)$ at $n$ becomes testing the original reduced coefficient at $nq_1\\cdots q_a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-inertia",
        "exact_quote": "For $p\\in U$, choose inertia $\\tau_p$ on which the new ramified quadratic character is $-1$, and an arithmetic Frobenius lift $\\phi_p$ in the same decomposition group. In characteristic zero, $$\\label{co:inertia-identity}\n U_p^{(2)}\\bigl(t(g\\tau_p\\phi_p)-t(g\\phi_p)\\bigr)\n   =d(\\phi_p)\\bigl(t(g\\tau_p)-t(g)\\bigr).$$ Here $U_p^{(2)}$ extracts $p$-multiples in weight two. The identity commutes with further good traces.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-co-symbol-interface",
        "exact_quote": "Under the arithmetic lower bounds in Lemma <a href=\"#co:minimum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:minimum\">50</a>, the symbols of either coefficient source have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For matching $U$, the trace on split elements factors through the maximal elementary $2$-extension of $F$ unramified outside $S\\cup U$. For opposite $U$, traces factor through the corresponding rational elementary extension. Adding a split vertex $q$ is read by coefficient $1$ of $t(\\operatorname{Fr}_q)H_U$; adding a simple vertex is read on the opposite $B_U$.*\n\n\n2.  *Let $p\\in U$ and $g=\\operatorname{Fr}_q$. Changing one oriented ramification bit at $p$ changes the symbol by $$\\label{co:contraction}\n     A\\bigl((U\\setminus\\{p\\})\\cup\\{q'\\}\\bigr),\\qquad\n     \\operatorname{Fr}_{q'}\\sim g\\phi_p,$$ unless $p,q$ are both simple, in which case the change is zero. The replacement has the product labels at all retained places. Its coefficient system has support $S\\cup(U\\setminus\\{p\\})$: the entire rational prime $p$, including both places above it when it splits in $F$, is omitted.*\n\n3.  *A split vertex with trivial label in the elementary extension for the other vertices has zero symbol.*\n\n4.  *In matching parity, insertion of two simple primes with equal rational labels on the fixed support and at the existing split vertices preserves the symbol if $\\#E(\\mathbb F_q)/2$ is odd for that label. Their mutual simple bits and their incidences with other simple vertices do not affect this statement.*\n\n*These are coefficient identities. Simultaneous realization of their labels by primes is a separate arithmetic construction.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gr-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many old vertices, with all their mutual data, and individually realizable new types. Every prescription of the independent forward bits involving at least one new vertex has a realization by distinct fresh primes, avoiding an arbitrary finite set. The old vertices and their mutual data remain unchanged. The same holds with further fixed local tests included in $S$. A contracted split cluster may be represented by the product of its oriented generators, with conjugations prescribed along its contraction tree; its support types and external residue bits are the corresponding sums. A cluster containing one simple vertex has the corresponding rational product data.*\n\n*For a symbol satisfying the omitted-vertex dependence in Proposition <a href=\"#co:symbol-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:symbol-interface\">54</a>, its value depends only on the graph data. Products in the preceding assertion may therefore be replaced by fresh prime representatives without changing the prescribed contracted symbol.",

        "anchor": "stmt-gr-address",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the binary symbols under consideration depend only on the arithmetic graph data and satisfy the contraction, whole-rational-prime omission, and primary-isolation rules of Proposition <a href=\"#co:symbol-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:symbol-interface\">54</a>, with the realization freedoms of Lemma <a href=\"#gr:realization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"gr:realization\">55</a>. Contraction includes the product labels and external incidences, and the zero rule for two simple vertices. These identities are required only on nonempty configurations and for contractions with nonempty output. Fix $b\\ge1$ and either of the following terminal arrangements.*\n\n1.  *One symbol, a nonempty set of always active terminals, and a nonzero polynomial in their independent pair bits.*\n\n2.  *One or two symbols on terminals with nonzero linear activations, including at least one terminal for every nonzero linear form. At each $x\\ne0$ each symbol separately is a nonzero polynomial in the active terminal bits. The symbols may read different compatible projections of a common graph alphabet and may have fixed stationary external data.*\n\n*Suppose also that at each nonzero address the total active terminal data, together with any stationary external data, obey the required filter. Starting from any fixed realization of the terminal and stationary vertices, one can adjoin finitely many fresh primary auxiliary vertices, activated by nonzero linear forms, and choose only incidences involving these new vertices so that every symbol equals $1$ at every $x\\ne0$. All data on the original vertices remain unchanged. No auxiliary is present at $0$. All auxiliaries may satisfy the fixed splitting and neutral external-incidence conditions incorporated into the primary type.*\n\n*It is enough that a terminal polynomial have a nonzero evaluation or a nonzero iterated edge difference. In particular a partitionability certificate suffices for the stationary hypothesis.",

        "anchor": "stmt-gr-forest",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the graph-symbol rules stated in Theorem <a href=\"#gr:address\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"gr:address\">57</a>. Differentiation in a tree of graph bits contracts the tree, adding product labels and external incidences; its value is zero if the tree contains two simple vertices. Differentiation in any collection of edge variables containing a cycle is zero, even when different edges use incompatible relative orientations. Two different oriented variables on the same pair count as a cycle of length two.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gr-network",
        "exact_quote": "A network acts on $P$ by a sum of finite-difference operators. A surviving term is a forest in which every component containing a primary vertex meets a terminal. A component meeting $t$ terminals acts by a contraction of degree $t-1$; a component containing two simple terminals is zero. Networks on disjoint sets of internal vertices, with no edges between those sets, multiply as operators on the same terminal polynomial. Consequently an operator of degree greater than $\\deg P$ annihilates $P$.*\n\n\n*Let $Q$ be the grounded Laplacian on the internal vertices: $Q_{ij}$ for $i\\ne j$ records the internal edge $ij$, while $Q_{ii}$ is the total incidence at $i$, including its terminal edges. Let $(c_a)_i$ record the incidence from internal vertex $i$ to terminal $a$. All entries are in $\\mathbf F_2$. The constant and pair-contraction coefficients of the operator are respectively $$\\label{gr:forest-coefficients}\n \\det Q,\\qquad c_a^{\\mathsf t}\\operatorname{adj}(Q)c_b\\quad(a\\ne b).$$ The second coefficient multiplies the terminal difference in the alignment selected by the network. A prohibited pair multiplies the zero operator.",

          "anchor": "stmt-gr-network",
          "exact_quote": "A network acts on $P$ by a sum of finite-difference operators. A surviving term is a forest in which every component containing a primary vertex meets a terminal. A component meeting $t$ terminals acts by a contraction of degree $t-1$; a component containing two simple terminals is zero. Networks on disjoint sets of internal vertices, with no edges between those sets, multiply as operators on the same terminal polynomial. Consequently an operator of degree greater than $\\deg P$ annihilates $P$.*\n\n\n*Let $Q$ be the grounded Laplacian on the internal vertices: $Q_{ij}$ for $i\\ne j$ records the internal edge $ij$, while $Q_{ii}$ is the total incidence at $i$, including its terminal edges. Let $(c_a)_i$ record the incidence from internal vertex $i$ to terminal $a$. All entries are in $\\mathbf F_2$. The constant and pair-contraction coefficients of the operator are respectively $$\\label{gr:forest-coefficients}\n \\det Q,\\qquad c_a^{\\mathsf t}\\operatorname{adj}(Q)c_b\\quad(a\\ne b).$$ The second coefficient multiplies the terminal difference in the alignment selected by the network. A prohibited pair multiplies the zero operator.",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-gr-programs",
        "exact_quote": "With the preceding grounding convention one has the following programs for every $b\\ge1$.*\n\n1.  *A network $\\mathcal S$ with $$\\mathcal S_{x_*}=\\Delta_z+O_{\\ge2},\\qquad\n     \\mathcal S_x=1+O_{\\ge1}\\quad(x\\ne x_*).$$*\n\n2.  *Two networks on the same activated sites, indexed by $\\varepsilon\\in\\mathbf F_2$, with $$\\mathcal N_x^{(\\varepsilon)}=1+O_{\\ge1},\\qquad\n     \\mathcal N_x^{(1)}+\\mathcal N_x^{(0)}\n     =\\begin{cases}\\Delta_z+O_{\\ge2},&x=x_*,\\\\\n     O_{\\ge2},&x\\ne x_*.\n     \\end{cases}$$*\n\n*All nonzero-activation sites are absent at $0$. Unspecified incidences, including those to stationary external vertices, may remain neutral.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pw-interfaces",
        "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for the fixed curve and fixed local data.*\n\n1.  *The cyclotomic missing-vertex statement of Theorem <a href=\"#cy:missing-vertex\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cy:missing-vertex\">5</a> applies to any fixed base $h_0$ of Selmer corank zero. If arbitrarily large binary cubes based at $h_0$, with no old prime removed, have nonzero central values at every other address and satisfy $$v(L_*(h_x))-2w(h_x)-s(h_x)\\le B\\quad(x\\ne0),$$ then the base central value is nonzero and satisfies the same inequality with $B$ replaced by $B+C$. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed curve and local data, not on $h_0$, its number of prime factors, or the cube dimension. The dimension required for the argument may depend on the base. The cyclotomic lower bound at an analytically nonvanishing parameter is also available.*\n\n\n2.  *Corollary <a href=\"#rd:ring-lower\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:ring-lower\">11</a> has the following uniform form. If $k<0$ is an odd fundamental discriminant with at most $A$ prime factors, $K=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$ is neither an extra-unit field nor the CM field, $k$ is a square at $2Nh$, and the twists $h,hk$ have respective analytic orders one and zero, then their ring-class Heegner sum has free-line index $j_h(k)$ satisfying $$\\label{pw:ring-lower-interface}\n     2j_h(k)\\ge 4w(h)+s(h)+s(hk)-C_{E,A}.$$ For a fixed such $k$, Theorem <a href=\"#rd:missing-vertex\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rd:missing-vertex\">12</a> applies to cubes based at $h=1$ whose new primes all split in $K$. If $s_2(E/K)=1$ and the nonzero addresses have product analytic order one and uniformly bounded $2j_h(k)-4w(h)-s(h)-s(hk)$, the base product has analytic order one.*\n\n\n\n3.  *The even coefficient system, exact same-filter square comparison, divided operators, and trace identities of Proposition <a href=\"#co:even\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:even\">46</a> and Lemmas <a href=\"#co:operators\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:operators\">51</a>, <a href=\"#co:traces\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:traces\">52</a>, and <a href=\"#co:inertia\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:inertia\">53</a> are available. After a uniform lower bound for the relevant point indices has been proved, the moving odd coefficient system and its stabilized minimum are available as in Proposition <a href=\"#co:odd\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:odd\">49</a> and Lemma <a href=\"#co:minimum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:minimum\">50</a>. The resulting unit symbols satisfy the contraction, good-twin, and primary-isolation rules of Proposition <a href=\"#co:symbol-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:symbol-interface\">54</a>. An even unit gives $$\\label{pw:even-unit-interface}\n     L_*(h)\\ne0,\\qquad v(L_*(h))\\le 2w(h)+C;$$ an odd unit gives analytic order one and $$\\label{pw:odd-unit-interface}\n     j(P_h)\\le w(h)+C.$$ The constants are independent of the number and sizes of the variable primes. The fixed genus partner used to define $P_h$ is denoted $h_*$.*\n\n4.  *Arithmetic realization and the forest and address statements of Lemmas <a href=\"#gr:realization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"gr:realization\">55</a> and <a href=\"#gr:forest\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"gr:forest\">58</a> and Theorem <a href=\"#gr:address\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"gr:address\">57</a> hold. In particular, separate nonzero terminal polynomials may be addressed simultaneously. Primary auxiliaries preserve all fixed local classes, have neutral incidence to specified stationary external vertices, and may be required to split in any compatible fixed fields. They contribute no prime at address zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pw-isogeny-corank",
        "exact_quote": "Recall that a rational isogeny preserves analytic rank, Mordell–Weil rank, and $2^\\infty$-Selmer corank. If $k$ is a quadratic discriminant and $K=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$, then $$\\label{pw:quadratic-corank}\n s_2(E/K)=s_2(E/\\mathbb Q)+s_2(E^{(k)}/\\mathbb Q).$$ For every $E/\\mathbb Q$ one has, separately, $$\\label{pw:kummer-corank}\n s_2(E)=r(E)+\n \\operatorname{corank}_{\\mathbb Z_2}\\operatorname{Sha}(E/\\mathbb Q)[2^\\infty].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pw-isogeny-corank",
          "exact_quote": "Recall that a rational isogeny preserves analytic rank, Mordell–Weil rank, and $2^\\infty$-Selmer corank. If $k$ is a quadratic discriminant and $K=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$, then $$\\label{pw:quadratic-corank}\n s_2(E/K)=s_2(E/\\mathbb Q)+s_2(E^{(k)}/\\mathbb Q).$$ For every $E/\\mathbb Q$ one has, separately, $$\\label{pw:kummer-corank}\n s_2(E)=r(E)+\n \\operatorname{corank}_{\\mathbb Z_2}\\operatorname{Sha}(E/\\mathbb Q)[2^\\infty].$$",
          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Recall that a rational isogeny preserves analytic rank, Mordell–Weil rank, and $2^\\infty$-Selmer corank. If $k$ is a quadratic discriminant and $K=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$, then $$\\label{pw:quadratic-corank}\n s_2(E/K)=s_2(E/\\mathbb Q)+s_2(E^{(k)}/\\mathbb Q).$$ For every $E/\\mathbb Q$ one has, separately, $$\\label{pw:kummer-corank}\n s_2(E)=r(E)+\n \\operatorname{corank}_{\\mathbb Z_2}\\operatorname{Sha}(E/\\mathbb Q)[2^\\infty].$$",
      "explanation": "Isogenies preserve the ranks, quadratic base change adds coranks, and the Kummer identity holds separately.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pw-minimum-seeds",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *In an all-split alphabet, suppose a unit symbol has a nonempty witness. At the least positive vertex count $r$ of such a witness, all mutual edge bits are immaterial. The symbol is determined by the individual fixed-support labels. This remains true if the empty configuration is a unit, provided no contraction to the empty configuration is used.*\n\n2.  *In the quadratic case, suppose the symbol has a nonzero witness, the alphabet is closed under the allowed contractions, and it contains the good twin operations. There are $r\\ge0$ and $s_1\\ge1$ such that for every $s\\ge s_1$ of the permitted simple parity, the minimum split count of a unit with $s$ simple vertices is $r$. At these counts all edge bits are immaterial. A chosen minimum seed may be padded by arbitrarily many good twin pairs.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },

There are $r\\ge0$ and $s_1\\ge1$ such that for every $s\\ge s_1$ of the permitted simple parity, the minimum split count of a unit with $s$ simple vertices is $r$.         "anchor": "stmt-pw-reserves",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a minimum seed and a finite label alphabet.*\n\n1.  *In the all-split rational alphabet, a list with the same total label as the seed and sufficiently many copies of its labels is partitionable.*\n\n\n2.  *In the quadratic alphabet, retain $r$ split anchors with the seed labels, and suppose the list and seed have the same total rational label on the projection used by the symbol. Suppose there are sufficiently many simple vertices of the required parity and sufficiently many split reserves of each realizable rational shift. If $F\\ne F'$, also suppose $$\\label{pw:donor-balance}\n     \\Delta=\\#\\{p:\\ p\\text{ split in }F\\text{ and inert in }F'\\}\n           -\\#\\{p:\\ p\\text{ inert in }F\\text{ and split in }F'\\}>C_0.$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on the anchors and the reserves. Then all other split vertices can be attached to simple vertices so that the result is the minimum seed and good twin pairs. The twins may match a prescribed finite enrichment of the rational labels. The base simple labels may be specified on any projection on which the required total agrees.*\n\n*All reserve requirements depend only on the finite alphabets, seed, and projection. There are constants $A,C$ such that every $h$ on this filter of analytic order one admits a negative odd fundamental discriminant $k'$ for which*\n\n1.  *$(k',2Nh)=1$, $k'$ is a square at every place of $2Nh$, and $\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k'})$ is nonexceptional;*\n\n2.  *$\\omega(k')\\le A$;*\n\n\n3.  *$L(E^{(hk')},1)\\ne0$ and $$\\label{pw:rough-bound}\n     v(L_*(hk'))\\le 2w(hk')+s(hk')+C.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-pw-height-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Here $j_h(k)$ is the index of the conductor-$|h|$ ring-class sum over $\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$, whereas $j(P_h)$ is the index of the conductor-one genus sum over $\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{hh_*})$. With the fixed modular parametrization and the fixed genus partner $h_*$, the explicit Gross–Zagier formula gives, whenever the relevant factors have analytic orders one and zero, $$\\label{pw:height-ratio}\n 2j_h(k)-2j(P_h)=v(L_*(hk))+O(1).$$ For $k$ with bounded prime count as in Lemma <a href=\"#pw:rough-companion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pw:rough-companion\">68</a>, the error is uniform in $h,k$; for a fixed $k$ it is uniform in $h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-004.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-pw-height-comparison",

          "source_id": "PM-004.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Here $j_h(k)$ is the index of the conductor-$|h|$ ring-class sum over $\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{k})$, whereas $j(P_h)$ is the index of the conductor-one genus sum over $\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{hh_*})$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **rank-quantities** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For an elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$, put $$r(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}A(\mathbb Q),\qquad a(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(A,s),\qquad s_2(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(A/\mathbb Q).$$
- **iwasawa-coefficient-rings** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Put $$\Lambda=\mathbb Z _2[[t]],\qquad \mathcal O=\widehat{\Lambda_{(2)}},\qquad \kappa=\mathcal O/2\mathcal O=\mathbb F _2((t)).$$
- **witness-and-partitionable-graph** ([source](#stmt-gr-partitionable)): A *witness* is a finite graph on which the symbol is $1$. A graph is *partitionable* for the symbol if its vertices can be partitioned into nonempty clusters, each with at most one simple vertex, with a choice of oriented contraction trees whose contracted graph is a witness. This definition includes a specified contraction certificate; it makes no assertion about a Selmer group.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-cyclotomic-lower-bound | two-adic valuation of the normalized central value | ≥ | $2w(h)+s(h)-C_E$ | constant independent of number and sizes of primes dividing the twist |
| q-binary-dimension | dimension of the Boolean cube | > | $r(2^M-1)$ | number of tests and precision, not the coefficients |
| q-ring-class-lower | twice the Heegner point index valuation | ≥ | $4w(h)+s(h)+s(hk)-C(E,A)$ | bounded prime count of the companion discriminant |
| q-residual-valuation | residual valuation of the determinant coordinate | ≥ | $-C(E,A)$ | depends on the curve and the bound on the prime count |
| q-minor-valuation | valuation of the determinant coordinate | ≥ | $-a-b$ | fixed clearing exponent and minor valuation bound |
| q-bit-threshold | dimension of the Boolean cube for prescribed coefficient bits | > | $q(2^M-1)$ | number of coefficient tests and precision |
| q-even-coefficient-bound | valuation of squarefree Fourier coefficient | ≥ | $w(D)+\tfrac12s(\sigma D)-C_g$ | constant independent of the index |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-ranks** [claim](#stmt-pr-ranks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pr-ranks): Direct limit of Kummer sequences and the forward theorem for low analytic rank. -> Selmer corank equals rank plus Shafarevich-Tate corank
- **s-invariance** [claim](#stmt-pr-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pr-invariance): Parity theorem, isogeny composites and restriction with conjugation projections. -> parity and quadratic corank identities
- **s-fourier** [claim](#stmt-cy-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-fourier): Fourier inversion and intersection of the completion with the localized ring. -> integrality over the power series group ring
- **s-binary** [claim](#stmt-cy-binary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-binary): Binary digit polynomials and the Chevalley-Warning parity count. -> nonzero address agreeing with the origin
- **s-perfect** [claim](#stmt-cy-perfect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-perfect): Finite free models over Artin quotients and inverse limit. -> perfect positive complex
- **s-residual** [claim](#stmt-cy-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-residual): Class group bounds along the cyclotomic tower and Kummer theory. -> concentration in one degree
- **s-central** [claim](#stmt-cy-central) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-central): Poitou-Tate lengths and the central specialization of the determinant. -> central valuation formula
- **s-cyclotomic-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cy-missing-vertex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cy-central-3): Congruence at a nonzero address contradicts an upper bound if the base value vanished. -> nonvanishing at the base
- **s-limits** [claim](#stmt-rl-finite-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rl-finite-limits): Ultrafilter limit of matrices over finite quotients. -> diagrams over the power series ring
- **s-kummer** [claim](#stmt-rl-kummer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rl-kummer): Free resolution of the completed point group and local duality. -> self-orthogonal Kummer condition
- **s-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-rl-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rl-evaluation): Evaluation matrices and cancellation of unit blocks in the cone. -> injection into crossed classes
- **s-kernel** [claim](#stmt-rl-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rl-kernel): Central scalar homothety argument and inflation-restriction. -> injective restriction to the exact kernel
- **s-outer** [claim](#stmt-rl-outer-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rl-outer-evaluation): Ultraproduct quotient as a discrete valuation ring. -> outer evaluation injection
- **s-derivative** [claim](#stmt-rd-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-derivative): Kolyvagin derivative operators with explicit integral descent. -> switch relation between coordinates
- **s-determinant-switch** [claim](#stmt-rd-det-switch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-det-switch): Compare determinant tensors of the two localization triangles. -> equal determinant valuations
- **s-outer-bound** [claim](#stmt-rd-outer-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-outer-bound): Outer rank reduction followed by a terminal minor estimate. -> uniform residual valuation bound
- **s-central-formula** [claim](#stmt-rd-central-formula) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-central-formula): Derived specialization and the isogeny identity for the quotient of invariants. -> central determinant valuation formula
- **s-family-clearing** [claim](#stmt-rd-family-clearing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-family-clearing): Schur elimination of singular blocks and clearing of denominators. -> bounded clearing factor
- **s-ring-transfer** [claim](#stmt-rd-missing-vertex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rd-binary-transfer-2): Congruence between the base and a nonzero vertex contradicts the nonzero-vertex upper bound. -> simple zero at the base
- **s-address** [claim](#stmt-gr-address) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gr-programs-2): Networks of primary vertices act as finite-difference operators selecting top coefficients. -> symbols equal to one at all nonzero addresses
- **s-even-base** [claim](#stmt-pw-even-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pw-even-base): Addressing with neutral packets and cyclotomic transfer. -> corank zero implies analytic rank zero
- **s-odd-base** [claim](#stmt-pw-odd-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pw-odd-base): Fixed even companion, simultaneous odd and even unit vertices, ring-class transfer. -> corank one implies analytic rank one
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pw-odd-base-2): Combine both base cases with the forward theorem for low analytic rank. -> the two-converse

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-binary-congruence**: For finitely many elements of the 2-adic group ring of the elementary abelian 2-group on b generators, and a precision M, if the dimension b exceeds r(2^M-1) then some nonzero character address x has every character value congruent modulo 2^M to its value at the trivial address.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-below-threshold**: Expect a nonzero address with matching coefficients when the cube dimension does not exceed the threshold. -> No such address may exist; a search can legitimately come back empty.
- **anti-non-integral-input**: Apply the congruence statement to elements whose coefficients are not 2-adic integers. -> The digit-wise Boolean-polynomial degree argument no longer applies, and congruences modulo a power of 2 lose their meaning.
- **anti-coefficient-dependent-bound**: Enlarge the dimension in proportion to the size of the group-ring coefficients. -> Wasteful and unfounded; the threshold has no such dependence.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The binary congruence lemma is a finite statement on a Boolean cube: for integer images of group-ring elements the smallest nonzero address with matching character values modulo a power of 2 is found by exhaustive finite enumeration.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens b M r, then r rows of 2^b integers (coefficients of z_j indexed by bitmask g)
# PRECONDITIONS: b>=1, M>=1, r>=1, each row holds exactly 2^b integers
# OUTPUTS: the smallest x in [1, 2^b) with (z_j)_x congruent to (z_j)_0 modulo 2^M for every j, else -1
# TIME: O(r * 4^b) character-sum steps
# SPACE: O(r * 2^b)
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, no floating point
# FAILURE: prints -1 when no such address exists; malformed input raises an exception
import sys


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    b, M, r = int(data[0]), int(data[1]), int(data[2])
    n = 1 << b
    mod = 1 << M
    pos = 3
    zs = []
    for _ in range(r):
        zs.append([int(v) for v in data[pos:pos + n]])
        pos += n

    def val(z, x):
        s = 0
        for g in range(n):
            if bin(g & x).count("1") % 2 == 0:
                s += z[g]
            else:
                s -= z[g]
        return s

    base = [val(z, 0) % mod for z in zs]
    for x in range(1, n):
        ok = True
        for j in range(r):
            if (val(zs[j], x) - base[j]) % mod != 0:
                ok = False
                break
        if ok:
            print(x)
            return
    print(-1)


main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a rank and finiteness theorem for elliptic curves over the rationals. It states no program, interface or data format that software could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no dynamical system, stochastic process or discrete-event model in the paper to simulate. The statements concern Selmer coranks and analytic orders of vanishing. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A computer algebra system that finds a 2-Selmer corank of at most one could cite the theorem as a certificate that rank and analytic order agree. The paper builds no tool, and the certificate holds only for rational two-torsion curves over the rationals. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is existential: it uses Chevalley–Warning parity, ultrafilter limits and Chebotarev density. It provides no finite procedure with stated cost, so no pseudocode or complexity is justified. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Arithmetic graphs and finite label spaces appear only as proof devices for contraction identities. The paper defines no data structure with operations or invariants meant to be stored. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, circuit or memory-bound computation is described. The 2-adic coefficient rings are used symbolically in proofs. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The curves are over the rationals and the theorem concerns global rank and the Shafarevich–Tate group. It says nothing about point counts or discrete logarithms over finite fields. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, model class, loss or learning guarantee. Its coefficient tests are exact arithmetic identities, not statistical estimates. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective function or feasibility problem. Sieve and network constructions serve only to prove that required nonvanishing tests exist. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communicating processes, consensus or fault model occurs. Simultaneous addressing refers to points of a Boolean cube, not to concurrent nodes. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nearest use is as an unconditional input to further work on the Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer problem. The paper itself states it as a converse theorem with no engineering contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-rational-two-torsion** (hypothesis): The theorem is stated only for elliptic curves over the rationals with a nonzero rational two-torsion point. Its constructions use the reducible residual range and do not cover curves with trivial rational two-torsion. Blocked use: ['Citing the result for a curve whose rational two-torsion is trivial', 'Using it as the broader two-converse without the two-torsion hypothesis']
- **limit-selmer-corank-at-most-one** (hypothesis): The conclusion needs the two-power Selmer corank to be zero or one. Nothing is asserted for larger corank. Blocked use: ['Inferring rank equality or finiteness of the Shafarevich-Tate group when the Selmer corank is two or more']
- **limit-selmer-corank-not-a-point** (formal_scope): Selmer corank one does not by itself produce a rational point of infinite order, because a divisible part of the Shafarevich-Tate group can account for it. Only after the theorem is the corank known to equal the Mordell-Weil rank. Blocked use: ['Substituting a finite two-Selmer dimension for the corank hypothesis', 'Searching for a generator on the strength of corank one before the theorem hypotheses are checked']
- **limit-no-leading-coefficient** (formal_scope): The result is a converse for ranks and finiteness. It does not give the leading coefficient formula, and a rank bound alone does not yield the Selmer hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem to certify regulators, periods or the exact order of the Shafarevich-Tate group', 'Deducing the Selmer hypothesis from a Mordell-Weil rank bound']
- **limit-cube-dimension-threshold** (edge_case): The binary congruence step guarantees a nonzero address only when the cube dimension exceeds a threshold that grows with the number of tests and the precision. Below the threshold no such address need exist. Blocked use: ['Expecting a matching nonzero address on a cube below the threshold', 'Treating the threshold as the cost of a search procedure']
- **limit-cyclotomic-base-corank-zero** (hypothesis): The cyclotomic missing-vertex transfer needs a base discriminant of Selmer corank zero and bounds at every nonzero address of arbitrarily large cubes. It does not handle a base of corank one. Blocked use: ['Applying the cyclotomic transfer to a base of positive Selmer corank', 'Assuming the transfer without the uniform bound at nonzero addresses']
- **limit-fixed-companion-field** (hypothesis): The ring-class missing-vertex implication fixes the imaginary quadratic field before the cube grows and requires Selmer corank one over that field. The bounded index estimate must hold uniformly at nonzero addresses, and no rank-one or finiteness assumption is made at the base. Blocked use: ['Varying the companion field with the cube dimension in the final transfer', 'Applying the implication when the Selmer corank over the field differs from one']
- **limit-ring-class-bounded-primes** (approximation): The ring-class lower bound holds with a constant that depends on the curve and on a bound for the number of prime factors of the companion discriminant. Without such a bound the constant is not controlled. Blocked use: ['Using the lower bound uniformly over companions with an unbounded number of prime factors']
- **limit-excluded-quadratic-fields** (edge_case): The ring-class constructions omit the two imaginary quadratic fields with extra units and, for curves with complex multiplication, the CM field. Results proved there do not apply to those fields. Blocked use: ['Taking the companion field to be one of the extra-unit fields', 'Taking the companion field to be the CM field of the curve']
- **limit-ultrafilter-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): Passing from finite precision diagrams to the Iwasawa algebra uses a cofinal subsequence or a nonprincipal ultrafilter. The limit is not given by an explicit procedure, and a later subsequence or other ultrafilter is not interchangeable. Blocked use: ['Reading the limit construction as an algorithm', 'Replacing the fixed ultrafilter by an arbitrary later one while keeping the retained evaluations']
- **limit-finite-cube-no-infinite-array** (formal_scope): The address statement is finite for each cube dimension. The number and size of auxiliary primes may grow with the dimension, so it does not provide a single infinite array of primes. Blocked use: ['Using the construction as one fixed infinite prime configuration', 'Quoting a prime size or count independent of the cube dimension']
- **limit-constants-fixed-while-realization-grows** (approximation): Only the finite realizations and their precisions change as the cube grows; the valuation constants stay fixed. This uniformity is claimed for the valuation constants, not for the sizes of the primes. Blocked use: ['Reading prime sizes or stage thresholds as bounded in the cube dimension']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a pointwise 2-converse for elliptic curves over $`\mathbf Q`$ with nonzero rational two-torsion: if the $`2^\infty`$-Selmer corank is zero or one, then the analytic rank and Mordell–Weil rank equal that corank, and the Shafarevich–Tate group is finite. The result allows arbitrary reduction at 2.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $E/\mathbb Q$ be an elliptic curve with $E(\mathbb Q)[2]\ne0$ and $s_2(E)\in\{0,1\}$. |
| [stmt-pr-ranks](#stmt-pr-ranks) | lemma | `pr:ranks` | 2 | There is an exact sequence |
| [stmt-pr-invariance](#stmt-pr-invariance) | lemma | `pr:invariance` | 3 | For every elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$, $$(-1)^{s_2(A)}=\varepsilon(A).$$ A rational isogeny preserves $a$, $r$, and $s_2$. |
| [stmt-cy-lower-bound](#stmt-cy-lower-bound) | theorem | `cy:lower-bound` | 4 | There is a constant $C_E$ such that, for every allowed $h$ with $L(E^{(h)},1)\ne0$, $$v_2(\mathcal L(h))\ \geq\ 2w(h)+s(h)-C_E.$$ The constant is independent of the number and sizes of the primes divi |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture predicts that the order of vanishing of the $L$-function of an elliptic curve equals its Mordell–Weil rank, and that its Shafarevich–Tate group is finite; its refined form also specifies the leading coefficient (Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer 1965; Tate 1966) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BirchSwinnertonDyer1965](#ref-BirchSwinnertonDyer1965), [Tate1966](#ref-Tate1966)]. For analytic order zero or one, the rank equality and finiteness are theorems of Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin (Gross and Zagier 1986; Kolyvagin 1990) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[GrossZagier1986](#ref-GrossZagier1986), [Kolyvagin1990](#ref-Kolyvagin1990)]. The converse problem begins with algebraic information and seeks the corresponding analytic order.

The appropriate algebraic hypothesis here is the corank of the full $2$-power Selmer group. It records a possible divisible contribution from the Shafarevich–Tate group as well as the Mordell–Weil rank. With the usual local Kummer conditions, put $$s_2(E)=\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(E/\mathbb Q).$$ Our main result is the following pointwise statement.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (The rational-two-torsion $2$-converse). *Let $E/\mathbb Q$ be an elliptic curve with $E(\mathbb Q)[2]\ne0$ and $s_2(E)\in\{0,1\}$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(E,s)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}E(\mathbb Q)=s_2(E),
\qquad
\operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)\text{ is finite}.$$*

</div>

The statement includes one rational two-torsion line and full rational two-torsion, CM and non-CM curves, every rational isogeny configuration, and every reduction type at $2$ and at the bad primes. It is a $2$-converse in this residual range. It does not assert the full Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer leading-coefficient formula, nor does a bound $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}E(\mathbb Q)\le1$ alone imply its Selmer hypothesis.

The broader companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[OpenAIGoldfeldTwoConverse2026](#ref-OpenAIGoldfeldTwoConverse2026)] proves the $2$-converse without the rational-two-torsion hypothesis. Here we give a separate full treatment of the rational-two-torsion case, developing its integral interpolation and finite twist constructions within this paper; the broader converse is not an input to the proof.

The converse with the stronger hypotheses $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}E(\mathbb Q)=1$ and $\operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)$ finite is known for every elliptic curve over $\mathbb Q$; see (Kim 2023, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kim2023SoftConverse](#ref-Kim2023SoftConverse)]. The fixed-prime problem asks instead whether a low Selmer corank alone forces the analytic order. Skinner and Wei Zhang established general rank-one converses at good ordinary odd primes under residual and local hypotheses (Skinner 2020; Zhang 2014) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Skinner2020](#ref-Skinner2020), [Zhang2014Heegner](#ref-Zhang2014Heegner)]. For odd Eisenstein primes, Castella, Grossi, Lee and Skinner prove the good-reduction converse under a local-character restriction (Castella et al. 2022, Theorem E) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CGLS2022](#ref-CGLS2022)]; Keller and Yin treat potentially good ordinary reduction without that restriction (Keller and Yin 2024, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KellerYin2024](#ref-KellerYin2024)]. Smith’s theorem gives the $50/50$ distribution of $2^\infty$-Selmer coranks in every fixed quadratic-twist family, counting twist parameters of both signs (Smith 2025) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Smith2025](#ref-Smith2025)]; this density statement does not decide a prescribed member.

The CM case includes pointwise results at the prime $2$. Burungale and Tian prove the rank-zero $p$-converse for CM curves at every prime (A. A. Burungale and Tian 2026) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BurungaleTian2026](#ref-BurungaleTian2026)]. In rank one, Burungale and Skinner treat good ordinary primes and, with a localization hypothesis, good supersingular primes (A. Burungale and Skinner 2024, Theorems A.1 and A.3, Remark A.5) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BurungaleSkinner2024Appendix](#ref-BurungaleSkinner2024Appendix)]. The earlier ordinary CM converse of Burungale, Castella, Skinner and Tian retains a Hecke-conductor condition (A. Burungale et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BurungaleCastellaSkinnerTian2022](#ref-BurungaleCastellaSkinnerTian2022)]. Kriz’s preprint gives rank-zero and rank-one converses for maximal-order CM curves at primes ramified in the CM field, including the pointwise $2$-converse for the congruent-number family (Kriz 2022, Theorems 1.1 and 10.17(2)) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Kriz2022CM](#ref-Kriz2022CM)]. These results already treat individual curves in their stated ranges; the theorem here includes non-CM curves with rational two-torsion and imposes no reduction condition at $2$.

The proof brings together two arithmetic interpolation constructions and a finite construction with quadratic twists. Kato’s zeta classes provide the cyclotomic input (Kato 2004) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Kato2004](#ref-Kato2004)]; Ferrero–Washington’s vanishing theorem controls the residual cyclotomic cohomology (Ferrero and Washington 1979) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FerreroWashington1979](#ref-FerreroWashington1979)]. Heegner points and their derivative classes provide the ring-class input, following the Euler-system method of Kolyvagin and its cohomological formulation by Howard (Kolyvagin 1990; Howard 2004) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Kolyvagin1990](#ref-Kolyvagin1990), [Howard2012](#ref-Howard2012)]. We retain integral local conditions and duality maps in the framework of Selmer complexes (Nekovář 2006) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Nekovar2006](#ref-Nekovar2006)]. The coefficient tests use the half-integral weight theory of Shimura and Waldspurger’s relation between coefficients and central values (Shimura 1973; Waldspurger 1981) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Shimura1973HalfIntegral](#ref-Shimura1973HalfIntegral), [Waldspurger1981](#ref-Waldspurger1981)]. Their ternary theta construction lies in the definite quaternion framework developed by Gross (Gross 1987) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gross1987Heights](#ref-Gross1987Heights)]; we use the Weil representation and the reduction description of Jetchev and Kane for the weighted Heegner-point tests (Weil 1964; Jetchev and Kane 2011) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Weil1964](#ref-Weil1964), [JetchevKane2010](#ref-JetchevKane2010)]. Each of these inputs has a precise role; none supplies the pointwise conclusion by itself.

Here is the common interpolation problem. Fix the curve that is to be tested, and index a finite family of quadratic twists by $x\in\mathbb F_2^b$, with $x=0$ denoting that curve. Each auxiliary prime divides the twist parameter according to a linear form in $x$. We construct these families so that every nonzero address has the required analytic order and a uniform normalized valuation bound. An integral interpolation argument then recovers the value or derivative at the missing address $0$. The number and sizes of the auxiliary primes may grow with $b$; the valuation bound may not.

For Selmer corank zero, the required nonzero values are central $L$-values. Their normalized valuations are measured by coefficients of half-integral weight forms. The cyclotomic determinant makes these values part of one integral family, and a congruence on a sufficiently large binary cube recovers the missing value. This also supplies the even estimate needed to normalize the rank-one construction.

The rank-one construction requires a further preparation. Fix a negative quadratic discriminant $h_*$ with $L(E^{(h_*)},1)\ne0$. For positive $h$ in the prescribed local squareclasses, the genus-character Heegner sum $P_h$ attached to $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{hh_*})$ detects a simple zero of $E^{(h)}$: its height measures $L'(E^{(h)},1)L(E^{(h_*)},1)$. To normalize the corresponding coefficient tests uniformly, we first bound the $2$-divisibility of $P_h$ from below in terms of the prime factors of $h$, with a constant independent of their number. For each parameter $h$ of known analytic rank one, a different negative companion $k'(h)$, with a bounded number of prime factors, combines the even estimate with a ring-class lower bound to give this uniform bound. Only after that argument do we normalize the odd coefficients and use their minimum-depth values as binary nonvanishing tests.

For the return to a prescribed corank-one curve, choose one final negative discriminant $k$ with $L(E^{(k)},1)\ne0$, and fix $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$. This companion does not vary with $h$ or with the cube dimension. The quadratic corank identity gives $s_2(E/K)=1$. We construct families with every new prime split in $K$ for which, at every nonzero address, $E^{(h)}$ has a simple zero and $E^{(hk)}$ has nonzero central value. The two coefficient tests together bound the normalized index of the ring-class Heegner sum over $K$. Ring-class interpolation then recovers the derivative at $h=1$. Thus $h_*$ defines the detector, $k'(h)$ proves its lower bound, and the fixed $k$ permits the final transfer.

The arithmetic difficulty is uniform integral control at the prime $2$. Residual irreducibility is unavailable here. On the cyclotomic side we use Kato classes from one fixed modular-form lattice, separating rational divisibility from uniform integrality. On the ring-class side we retain the full integral Kummer conditions at the bad places and actual maps from finite free models to Galois cochains. Heegner derivative operations preserve the determinant valuation even when a localized class is divisible by $2$. These constructions give the missing-vertex Theorems <a href="#cy:missing-vertex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cy:missing-vertex">5</a> and <a href="#rd:missing-vertex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:missing-vertex">12</a>.

The finite construction has a different task: it must make the required coefficient tests nonzero at every nonzero address simultaneously. The variables are quadratic residue symbols between auxiliary primes. Changing one such symbol contracts two prime labels; repeated changes are governed by forests. Finite networks of new primes then isolate one address at a time. For rank one, the derivative test and its central-value partner may be separately nonzero without taking nonzero values on the same assignment of residue symbols. Theorem <a href="#gr:address" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:address">57</a> adjoins prime networks making both tests equal to $1$ at every nonzero address.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-roadmap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-roadmap">1</a> records the order in which the valuation bounds are obtained and used. Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes the Selmer and local conventions. Section <a href="#cy:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cy:section">3</a> proves the cyclotomic interpolation statements; Sections <a href="#rl:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rl:section">4</a> and <a href="#rd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rd:section">5</a> construct the ring-class deformation and prove its bounds and transfer theorem. Section <a href="#sec:nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nonvanishing">6</a> supplies auxiliary twists with prescribed local behavior and a bounded number of prime factors where needed. Sections <a href="#co:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:section">7</a> and <a href="#gr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:section">8</a> give the coefficient tests and finite networks. Section <a href="#pw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:section">9</a> assembles these results at the prescribed curve.

```text
  A downward double arrow "$\Downarrow$" connects Box 2 to Box 3.
- Box 3: Contains:
  - "Fix $k$ with $L(E^{(k)}, 1) \neq 0$ and put $K = \mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{k})$"
  - Followed by downward transition text and arrow: "Odd and even tests + finite prime networks split in $K$" over "$\Downarrow$"
  - "Simple zeros for $E^{(h_x)}$ and nonzero values for $E^{(h_x k)}$ with a common normalized index bound, for every $x \neq 0$"
  A downward double arrow "$\Downarrow$" connects Box 3 to Box 4.
- Box 4 (bottom): Contains:
  - "Fixed-$K$ ring-class transfer and $s_2(E/K) = 1$"
  - Followed by a downward double arrow "$\Downarrow$"
  - "Analytic rank one at the original vertex $x = 0$"
```


<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Selmer coranks and fixed local data

For an elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$, put $$r(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}A(\mathbb Q),\qquad
 a(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(A,s),\qquad
 s_2(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(A/\mathbb Q).$$ The Selmer group uses the local Kummer image at every place. The analytic continuation and functional equation are supplied by modularity (Breuil et al. 2001) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BCDT2001](#ref-BCDT2001)]. Write $\varepsilon(A)\in\{1,-1\}$ for the root number.

<a id="stmt-pr-ranks"></a>
<div id="pr:ranks" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (The three ranks). *There is an exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow A(\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb Q_2/\mathbb Z_2
\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(A/\mathbb Q)
\longrightarrow \operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty]\longrightarrow0.$$ Consequently $$s_2(A)=r(A)+\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty].$$ If $a(A)\in\{0,1\}$, then $r(A)=a(A)$ and the whole group $\operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)$ is finite. In that case $s_2(A)=a(A)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pr-ranks"><summary>Proof of stmt-pr-ranks</summary>

*Proof.* The Kummer sequences for multiplication by $2^n$, with the local Kummer conditions imposed, give $$0\longrightarrow A(\mathbb Q)/2^n A(\mathbb Q)
\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^n}(A/\mathbb Q)
\longrightarrow \operatorname{Sha}(A/\mathbb Q)[2^n]\longrightarrow0.$$ Their direct limit is the displayed sequence. Taking $\mathbb Z_2$-coranks proves the identity. The analytic-rank-zero and analytic-rank-one assertions are the Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin theorem (Gross and Zagier 1986; Kolyvagin 1990) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GrossZagier1986](#ref-GrossZagier1986), [Kolyvagin1990](#ref-Kolyvagin1990)]; see also the precise unrestricted forward statement in (Skinner 2020, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Skinner2020](#ref-Skinner2020)]. ◻

</details>

No finite $2$-Selmer dimension will be substituted for $s_2$. In particular, $s_2(A)=1$ does not supply a rational point of infinite order: the exact sequence allows a contribution from the divisible part of $\operatorname{Sha}$.

<a id="stmt-pr-invariance"></a>
<div id="pr:invariance" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Parity and change of curve). *For every elliptic curve $A/\mathbb Q$, $$(-1)^{s_2(A)}=\varepsilon(A).$$ A rational isogeny preserves $a$, $r$, and $s_2$. If $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$ is quadratic, then $$s_2(A/K)=s_2(A/\mathbb Q)+s_2(A^{(k)}/\mathbb Q).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pr-invariance"><summary>Proof of stmt-pr-invariance</summary>

*Proof.* The $2$-parity assertion is due to Monsky (Monsky 1996) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Monsky1996](#ref-Monsky1996)]; see also (Dokchitser and Dokchitser 2010, Theorems 1.4 and 4.19) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[DokchitserDokchitser2010](#ref-DokchitserDokchitser2010)], which states the Selmer-parity result for every prime and credits Monsky for the case $2$. For an isogeny and its dual, both composites are multiplication by the degree. The corresponding maps on rational points and on Selmer groups respect the Kummer conditions. After rationalizing their Tate modules, the maps are inverse up to a nonzero scalar; hence the ranks and coranks agree. The equality of $L$-functions is isogeny invariance.

Over $K$, identify $A^{(k)}$ with $A$, and let $c$ be the nontrivial element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/\mathbb Q)$. Restriction and the two conjugation projections $1+c$ and $1-c$ define maps between the sum of the two Selmer groups over $\mathbb Q$ and the Selmer group over $K$ whose composites are multiplication by $2$. Functoriality of local Kummer images gives these maps at every place. Rationalization therefore gives the stated corank identity. This argument does not assert an integral direct-sum decomposition of the Selmer groups. ◻

</details>

Fix a curve $E/\mathbb Q$ of conductor $N$. A finite support $S$ always contains the primes dividing $2N$. It may be enlarged by finitely many primes when the fixed construction is prepared. A *local filter* consists of a real sign and unit squareclasses at $S$ for the variable fundamental discriminant. The filter is imposed on every relevant Fourier index, not just the squarefree indices. Its root number is constant. All variable primes lie outside $S$.

For an odd prime $p$, set $$p^*=\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)p.$$ Nonempty products of distinct $p^*$ are odd fundamental discriminants. We also admit the empty product $h=1$, which denotes the untwisted curve rather than a quadratic field. For a squarefree product $h$ of such factors, set $$t_p=\dim_{\mathbb F_2} E[2]^{\operatorname{Fr}_p},
 \qquad
 w(h)=\frac12\sum_{p\mid h}t_p.$$ In the rational-two-torsion range, $t_p$ is $1$ or $2$. Whenever the $2$-primary group below is finite, write $$s(h)=\mathop{\mathrm{length}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\operatorname{Sha}(E^{(h)}/\mathbb Q)[2^\infty].$$ Thus $s(h)$ is a finite length, whereas $s_2(E^{(h)})$ is a corank. In particular $s(h)$ is defined when $s_2(E^{(h)})=0$: the exact sequence in Lemma <a href="#pr:ranks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pr:ranks">2</a> and finite generation of the dual Selmer group make its $2$-primary quotient finite. This conclusion concerns the $2$-primary group; the low-analytic-rank theorem supplies finiteness of the whole group after analytic nonvanishing is proved. Let $v$ be a valuation above $2$, normalized by $v(2)=1$. After the coefficient field has been fixed, its value group is discrete.

The notation $L_*(h)$ denotes $L(E^{(h)},1)\sqrt{|h|}$ divided by the fixed real or imaginary period appropriate to the filter. The notation $\mathcal L(h)$ in Section <a href="#cy:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cy:section">3</a> denotes this same normalized value. Every modular parametrization used to form Heegner points is normalized to send the cusp $\infty$ to the identity of the elliptic curve. Changing among fixed nonzero modular parametrizations, isogenous curves, or period conventions contributes only bounded constants to valuations. Every such dependence is fixed before a binary cube is allowed to grow. Dependence on a growing number of prime factors will always be stated explicitly.

The following ledger records the order of choices used in the proof. It distinguishes bounds from the sizes and precisions needed to realize the auxiliary primes.

<div class="center">

| Data or bound                | Allowed dependence and order of choice                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|:-----------------------------|:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Fixed arithmetic constants   | The curve, its parametrization and periods, the fixed part of the support, and the chosen local classes; these are fixed before the varying prime support or cube dimension.                                           |
