This uses an isogeny identity for known finite groups, not BSD or converse finiteness at an unknown parameter.

<a id="stmt-pw-rough-companion"></a>
<div id="pw:rough-companion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 68** (A bounded companion for a varying odd parameter). *Fix a positive odd-sign filter, including the coefficient and class-group support used below, and a negative genus partner $h_*$ with $L(E^{(h_*)},1)\ne0$, chosen using Proposition <a href="#nv:local-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:local-witness">43</a> and then excluded from the variable parameters. There are constants $A,C$ such that every $h$ on this filter of analytic order one admits a negative odd fundamental discriminant $k'$ for which*

1.  *$(k',2Nh)=1$, $k'$ is a square at every place of $2Nh$, and $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k'})$ is nonexceptional;*

2.  *$\omega(k')\le A$;*

<a id="pw:rough-bound"></a>
3.  *$L(E^{(hk')},1)\ne0$ and $$\label{pw:rough-bound}
     v(L_*(hk'))\le 2w(hk')+s(hk')+C.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-rough-companion"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-rough-companion</summary>

*Proof.* At the fixed class-group support and the other fixed label tests, prepare an even negative-index symbol for each possible total type at this support, and for both isogeny orientations if needed. There are finitely many such preparations. The positive index $|hk'|$ has nonzero total rational type: its negative-discriminant parity is already nontrivial among the tests at $2$. Thus in the full-torsion case a unit seed has positive count. In the quadratic case use the stabilized seed of Lemma <a href="#pw:minimum-seeds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:minimum-seeds">63</a>.

When $F\ne F'$, interchanging the isogeny orientation interchanges the two counts in <a href="#pw:donor-balance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:donor-balance">[pw:donor-balance]</a>. Select the orientation with initial balance at least zero. The existing donors can then pay for arbitrarily many existing bad simples. They do not have to be supplied by new primes.

Let $R$ be the absolute prime-count bound in Proposition <a href="#nv:rough" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:rough">44</a>. Insert in $k'$ a finite dummy supply that meets the anchors, minimum simple count, and reserve requirements for every one of the finitely many prepared symbols. When $F\ne F'$, add donor slack paying for every reserved loss, the new dummy bad simples, and a further possible loss of $R$ from the later rough factor. When $F=F'$, all simple primes, including the later rough primes, are already good; only the simple and split-reserve supplies are needed. Add a fresh ramified prime if needed to exclude the exceptional imaginary fields. The number of dummy primes is bounded independently of $h$. Their labels and initial incidences may be chosen by Lemma <a href="#gr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:realization">55</a>.

Absorb $h$ and the dummy product into the curve. Apply Proposition <a href="#nv:rough" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:rough">44</a> to complete $k'$ by a squarefree factor coprime to all primes already used. Prescribe its sign and its local unit classes so that the final $k'$ is an odd negative fundamental discriminant, is a square at $2Nh$, and has the required original local classes at $2N$. These conditions give a nonempty reduced progression by the Chinese remainder theorem. Its root number is even: since $E^{(h)}$ has odd sign and $k'$ is negative and square at every prime of $2Nh$, the quadratic twist formula multiplies that sign by $\chi_{k'}(-N_{E^{(h)}})=-1$. Thus this is the positive-root-number case of Proposition <a href="#nv:rough" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nv:rough">44</a> for the absorbed curve. Additional fixed primes may be excluded. The proposition makes the central value at $hk'$ nonzero with at most $R$ new factors. Its size threshold may depend on $h$ and all these congruences; $R$ does not.

No particular resulting unit class at the auxiliary class-group support was assumed in advance: dummy supplies were prepared for every possible total there. For the actual total, Lemma <a href="#pw:reserves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:reserves">64</a> makes $hk'$ partitionable. Analytic nonvanishing gives Selmer corank zero by the forward theorem. Proposition <a href="#pw:partitionable-even" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:partitionable-even">66</a> therefore gives <a href="#pw:rough-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:rough-bound">[pw:rough-bound]</a>, with the isogeny comparison above if the other orientation was used. The finite preparation and the bound on dummy and rough factors give $A,C$ independent of $h$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pw-height-comparison"></a>
<div id="pw:height-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="pw:height-ratio"></a>
**Lemma 69** (Height comparison). *Here $j_h(k)$ is the index of the conductor-$|h|$ ring-class sum over $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$, whereas $j(P_h)$ is the index of the conductor-one genus sum over $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{hh_*})$. With the fixed modular parametrization and the fixed genus partner $h_*$, the explicit Gross–Zagier formula gives, whenever the relevant factors have analytic orders one and zero, $$\label{pw:height-ratio}
 2j_h(k)-2j(P_h)=v(L_*(hk))+O(1).$$ For $k$ with bounded prime count as in Lemma <a href="#pw:rough-companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:rough-companion">68</a>, the error is uniform in $h,k$; for a fixed $k$ it is uniform in $h$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-height-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-height-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Use the character-sum normalization of the explicit Gross–Zagier formula in (Cai, Shu, and Tian 2014, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-83"></a>[[CaiShuTian2014](#ref-CaiShuTian2014)]. The restriction of $\chi_h$ to $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$ has exact ring-class conductor $|h|$: each odd prime dividing $h$ splits and retains its ramified quadratic character. The genus character over $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{hh_*})$ is unramified, so the sum defining $P_h$ has conductor one. Their canonical heights are fixed modular factors times, respectively, $$|h|\sqrt{|k|}\,L'(E^{(h)},1)L(E^{(hk)},1),
 \qquad
 \sqrt{|hh_*|}\,L'(E^{(h)},1)L(E^{(h_*)},1).$$ In that theorem the multiplier is $c\sqrt{|D|}$, the level-discriminant factor is $2^{\mu(N,D)}$, and the unit factor is $[\mathcal O_c^\times:\mathbb Z^\times]^2$. Here every level prime splits, so $(N,D)=1$ and $\mu(N,D)=0$; the excluded extra-unit fields make the unit factor $1$ in both applications. Take their ratio. The derivative and the rank-one regulator cancel; the remaining varying real factor is $\sqrt{|hk|}$, precisely the factor in $L_*(hk)$. Comparison of the rational free line with its two quadratic projections has bounded index because their composites are multiplication by $2$. The modular parametrization and the factor involving $h_*$ are fixed. The level is split and the discriminant and conductor are prime to it; no growing conductor-prime product occurs in this normalization. Local differential and period comparisons have finitely many possibilities at the fixed support. The extra-unit fields have been excluded, and the number of additional places in the varying $k$ case is bounded. These observations give the asserted bounded valuation error. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pw-odd-lower"></a>
<div id="pw:odd-lower" class="proposition">

**Proposition 70** (Uniform lower bound for the odd detector). *Every analytically rank-one $h$ in the prepared positive filter of Lemma <a href="#pw:rough-companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:rough-companion">68</a> satisfies <a id="pw:odd-lower-bound"></a>$$\label{pw:odd-lower-bound}
 2j(P_h)\ge 2w(h)+s(h)-C.$$ In particular $j(P_h)\ge w(h)-C$ uniformly in $h$ and its prime count.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-odd-lower"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-odd-lower</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $k'$ by Lemma <a href="#pw:rough-companion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:rough-companion">68</a>. Combine <a href="#pw:ring-lower-interface" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:ring-lower-interface">[pw:ring-lower-interface]</a>, <a href="#pw:height-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:height-ratio">[pw:height-ratio]</a>, and <a href="#pw:rough-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:rough-bound">[pw:rough-bound]</a>. Since the factors are coprime, $w(hk')=w(h)+w(k')$, and $w(k')\le\omega(k')\le A$. Hence $$\begin{aligned}
 2j(P_h)
 &\ge 4w(h)+s(h)+s(hk')-v(L_*(hk'))-C\\
 &\ge 2w(h)+s(h)-2w(k')-C\\
 &\ge 2w(h)+s(h)-C'.
\end{aligned}$$ Only even symbols and independently nonvanishing companions were used. Thus this proof precedes, and does not assume, the odd minimum-depth normalization. ◻

</details>

<a id="pw:odd-stages"></a>
#### From the moving odd coefficients to finite graphs

Proposition <a href="#pw:odd-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-lower">70</a> now supplies the arithmetic premise of Lemma <a href="#co:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:minimum">50</a>. We obtain an odd symbol with fixed minimum normalized depth and the uniform unit bound <a href="#pw:odd-unit-interface" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:odd-unit-interface">[pw:odd-unit-interface]</a>. To use the finite seed and packet lemmas, we also need to stabilize its graph labels and values, not just this numerical depth.

Keep the ultrafilter used in Lemma <a href="#co:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:minimum">50</a>. The intrinsic fixed support includes the genus partner $h_*$; at an actual stage it also includes the detecting prime $\ell$. That prime adds at most two places in any fixed quadratic field. The unit and class-group description of $V_S(F)$ therefore bounds the dimension of the elementary label space independently of the stage. At $\ell$ the Schwartz function in Lemma <a href="#co:theta-bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:theta-bridge">48</a> uses only the quadratic residue-line character: increasing the Kummer precision does not impose a character of growing order on the variable primes. The old-support characters are fixed.

Choose bases and restrict to an ultrafilter-large set of stages on which the finite label spaces, their addition tables, conjugation and projection maps have one abstract form. At every fixed weight bound there are only finitely many typed graphs and symbol truth tables. Select the table occurring on an ultrafilter-large set. These selected tables are compatible under restriction and define one limit symbol. Its contraction and twin identities follow from the finite-stage identities. The witness sequence of Lemma <a href="#co:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:minimum">50</a> has bounded weight, hence takes only finitely many graph types in this alphabet; one type is a witness in the selected limit table.

If the empty configuration is a unit, a sufficiently advanced actual stage already detects analytic order one at $h=1$. Otherwise the limit witness is nonempty, so Lemma <a href="#pw:minimum-seeds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:minimum-seeds">63</a> supplies the minimum seeds and stabilized simple count needed below. Fix them in this remaining case; Lemmas <a href="#pw:reserves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:reserves">64</a> and <a href="#pw:packets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:packets">65</a> apply to their finite label alphabets. Every particular contraction or twin comparison uses only a bounded graph. Its selected truth table agrees with the actual symbol on an ultrafilter-large set of stages. Thus any prescribed finite collection of such comparisons holds at one sufficiently advanced stage.

Later we will add the fixed support of a separate companion and the label projection read by its even test. Once compatible lifts have been verified, we may stabilize this finite enrichment by restricting the *same* ultrafilter. This preserves the intrinsic odd symbol, its minimum, and its chosen seeds. The actual stage is selected only after the cube dimension and all its finite networks have been chosen: their maximum weight gives a finite list of table and precision requirements. No intersection over all weight bounds, and no single stage working for every cube dimension, is required.

<a id="pw:split-section"></a>
#### A nonempty split-field symbol with full two-torsion

We give the extra argument required when $E[2]$ is rational. It addresses the possibility that the usual even minimum is attained only at the empty configuration after restricting all new primes to split in the companion field.

<a id="stmt-pw-recurrence"></a>
<div id="pw:recurrence" class="lemma">

**Lemma 71** (Recurrence congruences). *Let $p$ be odd, $a\equiv p+1\pmod4$, and let $$b_0=1,\quad b_1=a-\kappa,\quad
 b_{s+1}=ab_s-pb_{s-1}.$$ If $\kappa=\pm1$, then $b_{s+1}-b_s$ is even and $$\frac{b_{s+1}-b_s}{2}\equiv\frac{a-\kappa-1}{2}\pmod2
 \quad(s\ge0).$$ If $\kappa=0$, put $b_{-1}=0$; then $b_s-b_{s-1}$ is odd for every $s\ge0$. Finally, for $\kappa=1$, if $a\equiv p+1\pmod{2^m}$ then $b_s\equiv p^s\pmod{2^m}$ for every $s\ge0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-recurrence"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-recurrence</summary>

*Proof.* Modulo $2$ the recurrence is $b_{s+1}=b_{s-1}$. Its initial values prove the parity assertions. For $d_s=b_{s+1}-b_s$, the recurrence for $d_s$ is the same. Directly, $d_1-d_0\equiv(p-1)(p-\kappa)\equiv0\pmod4$ when $\kappa=\pm1$. Thus $d_s/2$ is constant modulo $2$. The last assertion follows by substituting the sequence $p^s$ in the recurrence and checking its first two terms. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pw-split-witness"></a>
<div id="pw:split-witness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 72** (Nonempty $K$-split witness). *Suppose $E[2]$ is rational. Choose a negative unit $k$ of a fixed even symbol for $E$, prime to all previous fixed support and to the ramification support of $E[8]$. Put $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$, chosen nonexceptional. Let $S_0$ contain the complete fixed support of the coefficient system, $2N$, the primes of $k$, and primes generating the class group of $K$. On the positive filter of $1$ square at $S_0$, there is an even unit test on nonempty products of primes split in $K$ with these properties:*

1.  *it has a nonempty witness;*

2.  *it satisfies the oriented split contraction and primary-isolation rules over $K$, for contractions with nonempty output;*

<a id="pw:split-unit-bound"></a>
3.  *a unit at $D$ implies $$\label{pw:split-unit-bound}
     L(E^{(kD)},1)\ne0,\qquad v(L_*(kD))\le 2\omega(D)+C_k.$$*

*This conclusion uses the integral trace and local identities in Proposition <a href="#pw:interfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:interfaces">61</a>(3).*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-split-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-split-witness</summary>

*Proof.* If the ordinary coefficient test already has a nonempty split witness, there is nothing to construct. Otherwise we first obtain an additional trace congruence modulo $8$. That congruence makes one further division of the coefficient form integral. We verify its contraction identities, then use a prime inert in $K$ to force a nonzero test at a split prime.

<a id="pw:split-basic-depth"></a>
*Extra depth if the ordinary test has no witness.* Use the ordinary even coefficient form for $E^{(k)}$, normalized by $a(1)=1$. The positive filter is chosen so that $kD$ lies in the original negative filter. Since $k$ attains its least normalized depth, the exact ratio comparison of Proposition <a href="#co:even" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:even">46</a> gives $$\label{pw:split-basic-depth}
 v(a(D))\ge\omega(D)$$ at every allowed squarefree $D$. The factor $|k|$ cancels from this ratio; one is not subtracting unrelated bounded-error inequalities.

<a id="pw:split-extra-depth"></a>
If equality holds at a nonempty product of $K$-split primes, use the usual even bit, projected from oriented $K$-labels to rational labels. It has all the required properties by Proposition <a href="#co:symbol-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:symbol-interface">54</a>. We therefore assume for the rest of the proof that equality never occurs there. By the rational-phase assertion in Proposition <a href="#co:even" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:even">46</a>, normalization by $a(1)=1$ makes each nonzero squarefree coefficient a rational number times a root of unity. Its valuation is an integer, not just an element of the possibly ramified coefficient field’s discrete value group. Thus strict inequality implies $$\label{pw:split-extra-depth}
 v(a(D))\ge\omega(D)+1
 \quad\text{for every nonempty allowed all-$K$-split }D.$$

Write $g=\sum a(n)q^n$ for the form. Its characters satisfy $\psi/\eta=\chi_{-4}$, so $\eta$ is odd. For a set $U$ of $K$-split primes put $$B_U=2^{-|U|}\prod_{p\in U}(U_p-\eta(p)V_p)g,
 \qquad
 \Theta_J=\sum_{m>0,\ (m,S_0)=1}\eta(m)m q^{Jm^2}.$$ All expansions retain the prescribed support sieves. The ordinary divided form is $$H_U=\frac12\left(B_U-\sum_{J\subseteq U}B_U[J]\Theta_J\right).$$ Here $B_U[J]$ denotes its coefficient at the squarefree index $J$.

<a id="pw:split-mod8"></a>
*The resulting trace congruence.* We first prove $$\label{pw:split-mod8}
 a_p(E^{(k)})\equiv p+1\pmod8
 \quad\text{for every allowed good }p\text{ split in }K.$$ Apply the ordinary $H_{\{p\}}$ trace at a Frobenius lift $\phi_p$, testing coefficient $p^2$ after theta multiplication. The form $H_{\{p\}}$ is already integral, and $\theta\equiv1\pmod2$; hence theta multiplication does not alter any of its reduced coefficients after these divisions. On the conductor-one ramified principal-series part the identity is $$U_p^2t(\phi_p)=U_p^3+d(\phi_p)U_p.$$ On the unraised unary part it is the ordinary good-prime coefficient identity. These are the only two types in this expression. Consequently $$(t(\phi_p)f)[p^2]=f[p^3]+(d(\phi_p)f)[p].$$ Replace the trace by a fresh split prime $p'$ with the same finite data, including every support-unit test. At each rational quadratic character unramified outside $S_0$, a Frobenius lift at $p$ has the character value of $p$. Hence the matched $p'$ has the same value, and $pp'$ has value $1$. Equivalently, $p'$ has the same unit squareclasses as $p$ at all places of $S_0$, so their product is square at all of them, including $2$. Since $\phi_p\in G_K$, the new prime is also split in $K$. Its coefficient on the left tests $pp'$; by <a href="#pw:split-extra-depth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-extra-depth">[pw:split-extra-depth]</a> it is zero modulo the maximal ideal after the two divisions. The determinant term on the right is zero: the unary summand at $p$ has the same character and cancels there.

On the right, the indices $p$ and $p^3$ in $B_{\{p\}}$ have underlying squarefree index $1$; they involve only $a(1),a(p^2),a(p^4)$. The $J=\varnothing$ unary term has no coefficient at either nonsquare index. Thus no other squarefree coefficient contributes there. For $a=a_p(E^{(k)})$, the phase-free square coefficients are $1,a-1,a(a-1)-p$. Hence the remaining coefficient is, up to a unit, $$H_{\{p\}}[p^3]
 =\frac{a^2-2a-p+1-p(a-2)}4
 =\frac{(a-1)(a-p-1)}4.$$ It is zero modulo $2$. Full rational two-torsion makes $a$ even, so $a-1$ is odd; this proves <a href="#pw:split-mod8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-mod8">[pw:split-mod8]</a>. No individual total filter condition on $p$ was needed: its matched prime $p'$ makes $pp'$ lie on the filter, and the square-index terms on the right come from $1$.

<a id="pw:four-divided-form"></a>
<a id="pw:shell-valuation"></a>
*One further integral division.* Now define $$\label{pw:four-divided-form}
 \widetilde H_U=\frac14
 \left(B_U-\sum_{J\subseteq U}B_U[J]\Theta_J\right).$$ We verify integrality at every coefficient. Write a test index uniquely as $Jrm^2$, where $J\subseteq U$ and $r$ is squarefree and disjoint from $U$. The underlying squarefree coefficient is at $D=(U/J)r$. Removing the unit phases, a prime of $U/J$ contributes an odd factor $b_s-b_{s-1}$ with $\kappa=0$, and a prime of $J$ contributes $b_{s+1}-b_s$ with $\kappa=(D/p)$. Thus $$\label{pw:shell-valuation}
 v(B_U[Jrm^2])\ge\omega(r)$$ by <a href="#pw:split-basic-depth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-basic-depth">[pw:split-basic-depth]</a>, with one additional unit of valuation when $D$ is nonempty and all its primes split in $K$.

If $r\ne1$ and all primes of $D$ split in $K$, the last inequality is at least $\omega(r)+1\ge2$. If $D$ contains an inert prime, then $r$ contains at least two: the total rational $K$-character is trivial on the filter, and all primes of $U$ split. Then <a href="#pw:shell-valuation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:shell-valuation">[pw:shell-valuation]</a> is again at least $2$. No unary term has this squarefree part. This proves divisibility by $4$ for $r\ne1$.

Suppose $r=1$ and $J\ne U$. Then $D=U/J$ is nonempty and all split, so $B_U[J]$ is even. This cancellation can be checked without dividing by a leading multiplier, which may be zero. Factor from both shell coefficients the common phase and the scalar $2^{-|U|+|J|}a(U/J)$, whose valuation is at least $1$ by <a href="#pw:split-extra-depth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-extra-depth">[pw:split-extra-depth]</a>. The remaining factors are the odd differences at $U/J$, the integral divided differences at $J$, and the ordinary square-index factors outside $U$. By Lemma <a href="#pw:recurrence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:recurrence">71</a>, their product is congruent modulo $2$ to the product of their leading values, even when one of those values is zero modulo $2$. Since $m$ is odd, multiplication by $m$ preserves this congruence. The common scalar supplies the second factor $2$. Retaining the phase $\eta(m)$ therefore gives $$B_U[Jm^2]\equiv B_U[J]\eta(m)m\pmod4.$$ This is exactly the cancellation with $B_U[J]\Theta_J$.

Finally let $J=U$, so $D=1$. At a prime outside $U$ the recurrence gives $b_s\equiv p^s\pmod4$. At a prime of $U$, <a href="#pw:split-mod8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-mod8">[pw:split-mod8]</a> gives $b_s\equiv p^s\pmod8$, and hence $$\frac{b_{s+1}-b_s}{2}
 \equiv \frac{a_p-2}{2}\,p^s\pmod4.$$ Multiplying these congruences, again retaining the phases, gives $B_U[Um^2]\equiv B_U[U]\eta(m)m\pmod4$. This proves integrality in the last case and establishes <a href="#pw:four-divided-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:four-divided-form">[pw:four-divided-form]</a>.

For the trace argument we specify the integral module being used. Write $\psi_U$ for the character of $B_U$. The character of $\Theta_U$ is also $\psi_U$, so its diamond correction is zero. For the proper subsets the diamond formula is $$\langle u\rangle\widetilde H_U
 =\psi_U(u)\widetilde H_U+
 \sum_{J\subsetneq U}\frac{B_U[J]}2
 \frac{\psi_U(u)-\eta(u)(-J/u)}2\Theta_J.$$ Each $B_U[J]/2$ here is integral, and each divided character difference is zero or a unit. Let $\widetilde{\mathcal M}_U$ be the $\mathcal O$-module generated by $\theta\widetilde H_U$ and $\theta(B_U[J]/2)\Theta_J$ for $J\subsetneq U$, together with all their good-Hecke and diamond translates. Its generators are integral by this formula and the good-Hecke coefficient formula. The finite-module and Chebotarev-continuity argument of Lemma <a href="#co:traces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:traces">52</a> therefore applies to $\widetilde{\mathcal M}_U$. A fresh trace kills every displayed unary correction after coefficientwise reduction, also after further fresh tests. Thus the same coefficientwise reduced determinant and trace identities hold on the required orbit.

*The contraction and isolation identities.* On $\theta\widetilde H_U$, two fresh traces in $G_K$ give zero after reduction at every coefficient. To see this, start with an arbitrary test index $Jrm^2$ and choose the two fresh primes outside that index and the level. The filter imposes $\chi_K(D)=1$; since $U$ is all split, the number of inert factors in $r$ is either zero or at least two. The fresh traces replace $r$ by $rq_1q_2$, with both $q_i$ split. In the first case the valuation after division by $4$ is at least $\omega(r)+2+1-2=\omega(r)+1\ge1$. In the second case it is at least $\omega(r)+2-2=\omega(r)\ge2$. The unary terms disappear under these fresh tests. Additional fresh $G_K$ traces only improve these estimates, so vanishing holds on the entire reduced trace orbit. The trace identity therefore gives $$t(gu^2)=t(g)\qquad(g,u\in G_K)$$ on this reduced trace orbit. Thus the tests factor through the elementary extension of $K$ unramified off the level.

<a id="pw:deeper-bit"></a>
A single fresh $K$-split trace at coefficient $1$ reads the bit $$\label{pw:deeper-bit}
 \widetilde A(D)=
 \left(2^{-\omega(D)-1}a(D)\text{ with its phase removed}\right)\bmod2$$ for a nonempty all-split configuration. The contraction rule follows from the same integral local identity as in Lemma <a href="#co:inertia" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:inertia">53</a>. For completeness its first shell, with $p\in U$ and a fresh trace $q$, has underlying configuration $D'=(U/p)q$, which is still nonempty, and its reduction is $$c_p(D')\widetilde A(D'),\qquad
 c_p(D')=\frac{a_p-(D'/p)-1}{2}\bmod2.$$ Changing the oriented inertia bit changes $c_p$ once. The omitted-prime identity gives the product label and removes the entire rational prime $p$ from the new ramification support. Consequently its repeated contraction has precisely the forest interpretation of Lemma <a href="#gr:forest" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:forest">58</a>. An omitted-vertex label equal to the identity has trace zero, which is primary isolation. No contraction to the empty configuration has occurred.

*A nonempty witness for the deeper test.* It remains to prove that this deeper test has a witness. Fresh ramification gives $K\not\subseteq\mathbb Q(E[8])$. By Chebotarev there is a good prime $p$ inert in $K$ and trivial on the original $E[8]$. Then $$p\equiv1\pmod8,\qquad a_p(E^{(k)})\equiv-2\pmod8.$$ On $\widetilde H_\varnothing$, whose coefficient at $1$ is zero, test $t(u)^2$ at coefficient $1$, where $u=\operatorname{Fr}_p$. The square coefficient and matching unary character give, up to a unit, $$\frac{a_p(E^{(k)})-1-p}{4}\bmod2=1.$$ The determinant term at coefficient $1$ cancels. In characteristic two, $t(u^2)=t(u)^2$, so a single trace in $G_K$ is nonzero. A fresh prime realizing this finite trace test is split in $K$. Moreover $u^2$ is trivial on every rational quadratic character, so the prime lies on the required total rational filter. It is a nonempty witness for <a href="#pw:deeper-bit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:deeper-bit">[pw:deeper-bit]</a>. Finally the exact coefficient ratio gives <a href="#pw:split-unit-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-unit-bound">[pw:split-unit-bound]</a>, with the additional depth at most one. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pw-coinvariants"></a>
<div id="pw:coinvariants" class="lemma">

**Lemma 73** (Orientation correction). *Let $K$ be imaginary quadratic, let $S_0$ contain $2$ and its ramification support, and let $V_0$ be the elementary label space over $K$ unramified off $S_0$. Complex conjugation acts on $V_0$ by $c$. If the rational total of an oriented prime configuration is zero on every rational quadratic character unramified off $S_0$, then its oriented total belongs to $(1+c)V_0$. A reserved supply containing every label allows its orientations to be flipped to realize any prescribed change in $(1+c)V_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-coinvariants"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-coinvariants</summary>

*Proof.* Complex conjugation has order two outside $G_K$, so $G_{\mathbb Q}=G_K\rtimes\langle c\rangle$. A $c$-invariant quadratic character of $G_K$ extends by giving $c$ value zero. At a rational prime outside $S_0$, inertia lies in $G_K$ and the character is trivial there; thus its extension remains unramified outside $S_0$. The assumed total therefore annihilates $(V_0^*)^c$.

For a finite vector space with involution, the annihilator of $\ker(1+c^*)=(V_0^*)^c$ is $\operatorname{im}(1+c)$. This proves the first assertion. Flipping a vertex of label $v$ changes its oriented label from $v$ to $cv$, and hence changes the total by $(1+c)v$. Reserve one representative for every preimage needed to express the desired element of the image. Their independent flips give the second assertion. These flips preserve every rational norm label. ◻

</details>

<a id="pw:assembly-section"></a>
#### The corank-one original base

<a id="stmt-pw-odd-base"></a>
<div id="pw:odd-base" class="proposition">

**Proposition 74** (Odd original base). *If $s_2(E)=1$, then $a(E)=1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-odd-base"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-odd-base</summary>

*Proof.* Parity gives odd functional sign. Use the normalized odd limit symbol on the positive filter of $1$ from Section <a href="#pw:odd-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-stages">9.5</a>, with its fixed support, genus partner $h_*$, and chosen ultrafilter. If it detects the base, a sufficiently advanced actual stage proves the result. Otherwise choose its nonempty minimum seed. The moving detecting prime $\ell$ is not an extra fixed support requirement.

*Fixing the companion.* Choose a separate even symbol for negative discriminants square at $2N$, excluding the fixed odd support, the relevant torsion ramification, and the exceptional imaginary fields. Choose a unit $k$ of that symbol. Then $L(E^{(k)},1)\ne0$. Fix this $k$ and $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{k})$ for the rest of the proof, before choosing any cube dimension. Its fresh ramification makes it independent of the old fixed label extensions. At sufficiently advanced stages $\ell$ avoids $k$. The intrinsic odd label tests continue to omit the primes of $k$.

We construct common terminal packets, individually split in $K$, such that the odd terminal polynomial and an even terminal polynomial testing $hk$ are separately nonzero at each nonzero address. They need not have a common unit evaluation yet; the address lemma will supply that step.

*The quadratic residual case.* First suppose $F,F'$ are quadratic. Keep the original even symbol evaluated on $hk$, treating the vertices of $k$ as stationary external data. Enrich the rational labels of the $h$-vertices by the primes of $k$. Their total there and at the even support is required to be zero. Let $V$ be the resulting rational elementary label space, including the $F,F'$ and $K$ coordinates and all relations among these characters. The allowed simple labels form the affine set $\{v\in V:\chi_F(v)=1,\ \chi_K(v)=0\}$, in additive notation. The difference of any two of its elements belongs to $\ker\chi_F\cap\ker\chi_K$, precisely the realizable rational labels of the permitted split attachments. Translation by this subgroup is therefore transitive. The fields $F,F'$ are unramified outside the old support, whereas $K$ has a fresh ramified prime; thus $K$ is independent of their compositum. When $F\ne F'$, this realizes both the good-simple pattern $(1,1,0)$ and the donor pattern $(0,1,0)$ in their three coordinates. When $F=F'$, all simple labels are good.

For the old odd projection, take a normal closure of its finite label extension at any stage with $\ell$ avoiding $k$. It is unramified outside the old support and $\ell$. Once $\ell$ avoids $k$, a prime ramified in $K$ remains outside that set. Hence $K$ has trivial intersection with this normal closure, and every old odd label can be realized together with splitting in $K$. This applies also to oriented split anchors over $F$. The added stationary $k$ coordinates may have relations, but they are already included in $V$; they do not impose extra requirements on the omitted odd projection. These compatible lifts let us stabilize the common alphabet and its projection maps on the same ultrafilter, as in Section <a href="#pw:odd-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-stages">9.5</a>. The intrinsic odd minimum and seed are unchanged. Choose the following packets in this fixed abstract alphabet, before selecting an actual stage.

Use packets with sufficiently large simple supply and reserves in this kernel, and with donor slack when $F\ne F'$. For the even test, take no split anchors. If the fields are distinct, correct all bad simples to good ones; if they coincide, every simple is already good. Pair equal enriched labels, and retain a nonempty good base pair. Use reserves to match the pair and the remaining good pairs, and attach every leftover split vertex to the last base position. Its label is forced by total zero. The output is a collection of good twin pairs, each matching also at every split vertex of $k$. Twin insertion into the unit at $k$ proves that this output has even symbol one. Thus the even terminal polynomial is nonzero.

For the odd test, project away the newly added tests at $k$. The same packets contain enough split anchors, simple vertices and reserve shifts to contract to the stabilized odd seed and good pairs, by Lemma <a href="#pw:reserves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:reserves">64</a>. Its total on that projection is the prescribed zero total. Incidences between the retained anchors and the good pairs can be set after contraction, by independent edge choices. The odd polynomial is therefore nonzero as well. This odd test does not include the stationary vertices of $k$.

*Full rational two-torsion.* Now suppose the curve has full rational two-torsion. Use the even test of Lemma <a href="#pw:split-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:split-witness">72</a>, with $K$-oriented labels on its support $S_0$, and let $v_1,\ldots,v_r$ be a minimum nonempty witness. Its oriented total $v_1+\cdots+v_r$ lies in $(1+c)V_0$, by Lemma <a href="#pw:coinvariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:coinvariants">73</a>. This even test uses the oriented elementary extension of $K$, whereas the odd test reads only its rational projection; the even contraction rule is needed only for nonempty outputs. Every odd seed label lifts to a $K$-split prime because $K$ was freshly ramified outside its intrinsic support. Each even label also has a compatible lift to the common alphabet. Stabilize the finite common alphabet and projections on the same ultrafilter, preserving the intrinsic odd symbol as in Section <a href="#pw:odd-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-stages">9.5</a>.

Choose packets containing ample copies of every needed even label, all odd seed labels on their rational projection, and orientation-shifting reserves. Arbitrary pairs of projected labels need not lift: take a union of sufficiently many compatible lifts for each projection separately. Duplicate each full label, so the packet has rational total zero.

For the even contraction, retain anchors with labels $v_1,\ldots,v_{r-1}$. After reserving these anchors, use orientation flips to make the total equal to $\sum v_i$; this is possible by Lemma <a href="#pw:coinvariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:coinvariants">73</a>. In a contraction certificate these flips are choices of conjugations along the forest, as permitted by Lemma <a href="#gr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:realization">55</a>; they do not change the common terminal data separately at different addresses. Merge all remaining vertices into the last cluster. Its label is $v_r$. The cluster is nonempty, and the minimum nonempty count makes the output a unit independently of its mutual edges. For the odd contraction, use its rational projection. Retain all but the last odd seed label and merge the remainder. Total zero gives the last label, so this is also a unit. Orientation corrections for the even test did not change these rational labels.

*Simultaneous addressing and an actual stage.* Now fix $b$ and put a sufficient common packet at every nonzero linear form on $\mathbb F_2^b$. Each nonzero address has two separately nonzero terminal polynomials. In the fixed abstract alphabet, Theorem <a href="#gr:address" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:address">57</a> gives finite primary-auxiliary networks making both symbols one there. Require their primary types to split in $K$ (and $F$ in the quadratic case) and to be neutral at the stationary primes of $k$. They are primary at every support coordinate used by either projection, so both symbol identities apply to the same common graph data.

Let $W_b$ bound all graph weights required by these finite networks. A finite intersection of ultrafilter-large sets supplies an actual stage with the same truth table through $W_b$, all required divisibilities and trace identities, and sufficient precision for the prescribed divisions. The moving prime also avoids the fixed companion $k$. Realize the resulting finite graph at that stage using Lemma <a href="#gr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:realization">55</a>. All active products $h$ are positive and square at the fixed tests, and all their primes split in $K$. No auxiliary appears at address zero. The depth constant and fixed companion remain unchanged as $b$ grows.

*Returning to the original curve.* At every nonzero address the analytic orders of $E^{(h)}$ and $E^{(hk)}$ are respectively one and zero. The odd unit bound, the even unit bound (or <a href="#pw:split-unit-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:split-unit-bound">[pw:split-unit-bound]</a>), and Lemma <a href="#pw:height-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:height-comparison">69</a> with fixed $k$ give $$2j_h(k)\le 4w(h)+C.$$ The finite Sha lengths at these addresses are nonnegative, so this also bounds $2j_h(k)-4w(h)-s(h)-s(hk)$ from above.

At address zero, $L(E^{(k)},1)\ne0$ gives $s_2(E^{(k)})=0$ by the forward theorem. Lemma <a href="#pw:isogeny-corank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:isogeny-corank">62</a> therefore gives $s_2(E/K)=1$. Ring-class missing-vertex transfer now makes the base product $L(E,s)L(E^{(k)},s)$ have a simple zero. Its second factor is nonzero at $1$, and hence $a(E)=1$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-pw-odd-base-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-pw-odd-base</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Use Lemma <a href="#pw:isogeny-corank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:isogeny-corank">62</a> to choose the indicated isogeny orientation before constructing either coefficient system. The even coefficient system and cyclotomic transfer give both Proposition <a href="#pw:partitionable-even" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:partitionable-even">66</a> and the corank-zero conclusion of Proposition <a href="#pw:even-base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:even-base">67</a>. The bounded varying companion, ring-class lower bound, and height comparison then establish Proposition <a href="#pw:odd-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-lower">70</a>. This discharges the arithmetic premise before the odd normalization and finite-graph construction of Section <a href="#pw:odd-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-stages">9.5</a>.

For the corank-one case, Proposition <a href="#pw:odd-base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pw:odd-base">74</a> fixes a nonvanishing even companion before the cube dimension, constructs simultaneous unit vertices, and applies ring-class transfer at the original base. This gives $a(E)=s_2(E)$ in both cases. The established Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin implication in analytic rank zero or one gives $r(E)=a(E)$ and finiteness of the whole Shafarevich–Tate group. Equation <a href="#pw:kummer-corank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:kummer-corank">[pw:kummer-corank]</a> remains the underlying corank identity throughout. In particular, neither a finite two-Selmer dimension nor Mordell–Weil rank was substituted for the original Selmer corank hypothesis. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pw-status"></a>
<div id="pw:status" class="remark">

*Remark 75* (The prescribed base). The final companion has Selmer corank zero because it was chosen with nonzero central value. Thus <a href="#pw:quadratic-corank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pw:quadratic-corank">[pw:quadratic-corank]</a> supplies the required corank over $K$; it is not an additional companion hypothesis. Only the finite realizations and their precisions change as the cube grows. The valuation constants remain fixed, so transfer applies to the prescribed original curve without deleting a density-zero set of bases.

</div>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Even estimate $⇓$ $⇓$ $⇓$ The even construction proves the corank-zero case and a uniform estimate; the latter supplies the bound needed to normalize the odd coefficients. The detector partner $h_{*}$ is fixed, the bounded-factor companion $k′(h)$ varies only in the lower-bound step, and the final companion $k$ is fixed before the simultaneous families are constructed. The families and auxiliary primes may grow with the cube dimension; the normalized index bound may not.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-roadmap",
      "pdf_page": 4,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:proof-roadmap",
          "exact_quote": "The even construction proves the corank-zero case and a uniform\nestimate; the latter supplies the bound needed to normalize the odd coefficients.\nThe detector partner $h_*$ is fixed, the bounded-factor companion $k'(h)$\nvaries only in the l",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "A vertical flowchart structured into four rectangular boxes arranged from top to bottom, connected by downward arrows:\n- Box 1 (top): Contains the title text \"Even coefficients, finite prime networks and cyclotomic transfer\", from which two diagonal arrows diverge:\n  - A down-left arrow ($\\swarrow$) pointing to \"Uniform even estimate (Proposition 9.6)\"\n  - A down-right arrow ($\\searrow$) pointing to \"Corank-zero conclusion (Proposition 9.7)\"\n  Beneath \"Uniform even estimate (Proposition 9.6)\", a downward double arrow labeled \"Even estimate $\\Downarrow$\" exits the bottom border of the box.\n- Box 2: Contains:\n  - \"Fix $h_*$ to define $P_h$; choose a bounded-factor companion $k'(h)$\"\n  - Followed by downward transition text and arrow: \"Even estimate + ring-class lower bound\" over \"$\\Downarrow$\"\n  - \"Uniform lower bound for the 2-divisibility of $P_h$ and odd coefficient normalization\"\n  A downward double arrow \"$\\Downarrow$\" connects Box 2 to Box 3.\n- Box 3: Contains:\n  - \"Fix $k$ with $L(E^{(k)}, 1) \\neq 0$ and put $K = \\mathbb{Q}(\\sqrt{k})$\"\n  - Followed by downward transition text and arrow: \"Odd and even tests + finite prime networks split in $K$\" over \"$\\Downarrow$\"\n  - \"Simple zeros for $E^{(h_x)}$ and nonzero values for $E^{(h_x k)}$ with a common normalized index bound, for every $x \\neq 0$\"\n  A downward double arrow \"$\\Downarrow$\" connects Box 3 to Box 4.\n- Box 4 (bottom): Contains:\n  - \"Fixed-$K$ ring-class transfer and $s_2(E/K) = 1$\"\n  - Followed by a downward double arrow \"$\\Downarrow$\"\n  - \"Analytic rank one at the original vertex $x = 0$\""
    }
  ],
